<!-- defn-1: body_md -->
\label{defn:DiagonalReflection}
Let $\lambda$ be a regular cardinal. If $S\subseteq\lambda$ is stationary in $\lambda$, then an ordinal $\rho<\lambda$ of uncountable cofinality is called a \emph{reflection point} of $S$ if $S\cap\rho$ is stationary in $\rho$. If $\mathcal{S}$ is a set of stationary subsets of $\lambda$, then an ordinal $\rho$ is a \emph{simultaneous reflection point} of $\mathcal{S}$ if it is a reflection point of every $S\in\mathcal{S}$. It is an \emph{exact reflection point} of $\mathcal{S}$ if $\rho\cap\bigcup{\mathcal{S}}$ contains a club (equivalently, $\rho$ is not a reflection point of $\lambda\setminus\bigcup\mathcal{S}$.) Further, it is a \emph{strongly exact reflection point} of $\mathcal{S}$ if for every $T\subseteq\rho$ which is stationary in $\rho$, there is an $S\in\mathcal{S}$ with $T\cap S$ stationary in $\rho$. Clearly, a strongly exact reflection point is an exact reflection point.

Let $S$ be a stationary subset of some regular cardinal $\lambda$, and let $\kappa$ be a cardinal.

The \emph{simultaneous reflection principle} $\ensuremath{\mathsf{Refl}}(\kappa,S)$ says that whenever $\mathcal{S}$ is a set of $\kappa$ many stationary subsets of $S$, then $\mathcal{S}$ has a simultaneous reflection point. The \emph{(strongly) exact simultaneous reflection principle,} denoted \emph{(strongly) exact} $\ensuremath{\mathsf{Refl}}(\kappa,S)$, postulates the existence of a (strongly) exact simultaneous reflection point in this situation.

The \emph{diagonal reflection principle} $\mathsf{DSR}({<}\kappa,S)$ says that if $\langle S_{\alpha,\xi}\;|\;\alpha<\lambda,\ \xi<\zeta_\alpha\rangle$ is a matrix of stationary subsets of $S$, where $\zeta_\alpha<\kappa$ for every $\alpha<\lambda$, then there are an ordinal $\rho<\lambda$ of uncountable cofinality and a set $R\subseteq\rho$ which contains a club in $\rho$, such that $\rho$ is a simultaneous reflection point of the set $\vec{S}|R=\{S_{\alpha,\xi}\;|\;\alpha\in R,\ \xi<\zeta_\alpha\}$. The \emph{(strongly) exact diagonal reflection principle}, denoted \emph{(strongly) exact} $\mathsf{DSR}({<}\kappa,S)$, further requires that $\rho$ is a (strongly) exact reflection point of $\vec{S}|R$.

The versions of these principles in which $j_\alpha\le\kappa$ are denoted $\mathsf{DSR}(\kappa,S)$, (strongly) exact $\mathsf{DSR}(\kappa,S)$, respectively.

If $R$ is only required to be \emph{unbounded} in $\rho$, then the resulting principles are called $\mathsf{uDSR}({<}\kappa,S)$, (strongly) exact $\mathsf{uDSR}({<}\kappa,S)$,
and if it is required to be \emph{stationary,} then they are denoted $\ensuremath{\mathsf{sDSR}}({<}\kappa,S)$, (strongly) exact $\ensuremath{\mathsf{sDSR}}({<}\kappa,S)$, respectively.
<!-- end defn-1 -->
<!-- fact-1: body_md -->
\label{fact:PointOfExactness}
If $\lambda>\kappa$ are regular cardinals and $S\subseteq\lambda$ is stationary in $\lambda$ such that exact $\ensuremath{\mathsf{Refl}}(\kappa,S)$ holds, then $\lambda^\kappa=\lambda$.
<!-- end fact-1 -->
<!-- notation-1: body_md -->
For a regular cardinal $\kappa$ and a limit ordinal $\lambda>\kappa$, we write
\[S^\lambda_\kappa=\{\alpha<\lambda\;|\; {\rm cf} (\alpha)=\kappa\}.\]
<!-- end notation-1 -->
<!-- defn-2: body_md -->
\label{def:OSR}
The principle $\ensuremath{\mathsf{OSR}}\xspace_{\omega_2}$ (for ``ordinal stationary reflection'') states that whenever ${\vec{S}}={\langleS_\alpha\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha<\omega_2\rangle}$ is a sequence of stationary subsets of $S^{\omega_2}_\omega$, there is an ordinal $\rho<\omega_2$ of uncountable cofinality which is a simultaneous reflection point of $\{S_\alpha\;|\; \alpha<\rho\}$. The principle \emph{(strongly) exact} $\ensuremath{\mathsf{OSR}}\xspace_{\omega_2}$ requires $\rho$ to be a (strongly) exact reflection point in this situation.
<!-- end defn-2 -->
<!-- defn-3: body_md -->
\label{defn:ProjectionsAndLiftings}
Let $\kappa$ be a regular cardinal, and let $S\subseteq\kappa$ be unbounded. Let $\kappa\subseteq A$. Then
\[\mathsf{lifting}(S,[A]^\omega)=\{x\in[A]^\omega\;|\;\sup(x\cap\kappa)\in S \}\]
is the \emph{lifting} of $S$ to $[A]^\omega$.

Now suppose $\omega_1 \subseteq B_0 \subseteq A \subseteq B_1$ and $X\subseteq[A]^\omega$ is stationary. Then we define the projections of $X$ to $[B_0]^\omega$ and $[B_1]^\omega$ by
\begin{gather*}
X\mathbin{\downarrow}[B_0]^\omega=\{x\cap B_0 \;|\; x\in X\} , \\
X\mathbin{\uparrow}[B_1]^\omega=\{y\in[B_1]^\omega\;|\; y\cap A\in X\} .
\end{gather*}
<!-- end defn-3 -->
<!-- defn-4: body_md -->
\label{def:P_S}
Let $H$ be an uncountable set, and let $X\subseteq[H]^\omega$. Then
${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ is the forcing notion consisting of conditions of the form $p={\langleM^p_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi\le \zeta^p\rangle}$, with $\zeta^p<\omega_1$, which are increasing continuous $\in$-chains through $X$, and ordered by end-extension.
<!-- end defn-4 -->
<!-- fact-2: body_md -->
\label{fact:P_SisCountablyDistributiveAndExhaustive}
Let $\kappa$ be a regular uncountable cardinal, and let $X\subseteq[H_\kappa]^\omega$ be stationary in $[H_\kappa]^\omega$. Then
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\arabic*)}]
  \item
  \label{item:GenericSequenceHasLengthOmega1}
  for every countable ordinal $\zeta$, the set of conditions $p$ with $\zeta^p\ge\zeta$ is dense in ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$,
  \item
  \label{item:UnionOfGenericIsExhaustive}
  for every $a\in H_\kappa$, the set of conditions $p$ such that there is a $\xi\le\zeta^p$ with $a\in M^p_\xi$ is dense in ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$,
  \item
  \label{item:CountablyDistributive}
  ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ is countably distributive.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end fact-2 -->
<!-- defn-5: body_md -->
\label{def:FragmentsOfSRPandForcingAxioms}
For a class $\Gamma$ of forcing notions, the \emph{$\Gamma$-fragment of the strong reflection principle,} denoted $\Gamma$-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace$, states that whenever $\kappa>\omega_1$ is a regular cardinal and $X\subseteq[H_\kappa]^\omega$ is stationary and ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X\in\Gamma$, then there is an increasing continuous $\in$-chain of length $\omega_1$ through $X$.

The strong reflection principle, denoted \ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace, is $\Gamma$-\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace where $\Gamma$ is the class of all stationary set preserving forcing notions. And, fixing a regular cardinal $\kappa$, $\Gamma$-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace(\kappa)$ is formulated like $\Gamma$-\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace, but only for stationary sets $X\subseteq[H_\kappa]^\omega$.

We also write $\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}_{\omega_1}(\Gamma)$ for the forcing axiom for $\Gamma$ stating that given any forcing notion ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}\in\Gamma$ and any collection $\mathcal{D}$ of up to $\omega_1$ many dense subsets of ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$, there is a filter in ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ meeting each set in $\mathcal{D}$. Martin's Maximum, introduced in \cite{FMS:MM1}, is the case where $\Gamma$ consists of all stationary set preserving forcing notions.

A strengthening of this forcing axiom, denoted $\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}^{++}(\Gamma)$, states that, given ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}\in\Gamma$, a collection $\mathcal{D}$ as before, and a set $\mathcal{T}$ of up to $\omega_1$ many ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$-names such that for every $\dot{T}\in\mathcal{T}$, $\Vdash_{\mathord{\mathbb P}}$``$\dot{T}$ is a stationary subset of $\omega_1$,'' there is a filter $F$ in ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ which meets each member of $\mathcal{D}$ and interprets each $\dot{T}\in\mathcal{T}$ as a stationary subset of $\omega_1$, that is $\{\xi<\omega_1\;|\;\exists p\in F\quad p\Vdash\check{\xi}\in\dot{T}\}$ is stationary.
<!-- end defn-5 -->
<!-- fact-3: body_md -->
\label{fact:LarsonsSeparation}
$\ensuremath{\mathsf{MM}}\xspace$ implies $\ensuremath{\mathsf{OSR}}\xspace_{\omega_2}$, but assuming the consistency of $\ensuremath{\mathsf{MM}}\xspace$, $\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace+\neg\ensuremath{\mathsf{OSR}}\xspace_{\omega_2}$ is consistent.
<!-- end fact-3 -->
<!-- defn-6: body_md -->
\label{def:Full}
A transitive model $\mathcal{N}$ of \ensuremath{{\ensuremath{\mathsf{ZFC}}\xspace}^-}\xspace is \emph{full} if there is an ordinal $\gamma>0$ such that $L_\gamma(\mathcal{N})\models\ensuremath{{\ensuremath{\mathsf{ZFC}}\xspace}^-}\xspace$ and $\mathcal{N}$ is regular in $L_\gamma(\mathcal{N})$, meaning that if $a\in \mathcal{N}$, $f:a\longrightarrow \mathcal{N}$ and $f\in L_\gamma(\mathcal{N})$, then $ {\rm ran} (f)\in \mathcal{N}$. A possibly nontransitive but well-founded model of \ensuremath{{\ensuremath{\mathsf{ZFC}}\xspace}^-}\xspace is full if its transitive isomorph is full.
<!-- end defn-6 -->
<!-- defn-7: body_md -->
\label{def:(infinity-)subcompleteness}
A forcing notion ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ is $\infty$-\emph{subcomplete} if every sufficiently large cardinal $\theta$ \emph{verifies} the $\infty$-subcompleteness of ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$, which means that ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}\in H_\theta$, and for any \ensuremath{{\ensuremath{\mathsf{ZFC}}\xspace}^-}\xspace{} model $\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{L}_\tau^A$ with $\theta<\tau$ and $H_\theta\subseteq \mathcal{N}$, any $\sigma:\bar{\mathcal{N}}\prec \mathcal{N}$ such that $\bar{\mathcal{N}}$ is countable, transitive and full and such that ${\mathord{\mathbb P}},\theta\in {\rm ran} (\sigma)$, any $\bar{G}\subseteq\bar{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}}$ which is $\bar{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}}$-generic over $\bar{\mathcal{N}}$, any $\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{N}}$, the following holds. Letting $\sigma( {\langle \bar{\theta},\bar{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}} \rangle} )= {\langle \theta,{\mathord{\mathbb P}} \rangle} $, there is a condition $p\in{\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ such that whenever $G\subseteq{\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ is ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$-generic over $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$ with $p\in G$, there is in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]$ a $\sigma':\bar{\mathcal{N}}\prec \mathcal{N}$ such that
	\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
                                     
                                                   
		\item
        \label{item:AgreementOnParameters}
        $\sigma'( {\langle \bar{a},\bar{\theta},\bar{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}} \rangle} )=\sigma( {\langle \bar{a},\bar{\theta},\bar{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}} \rangle} )$.
		\item
        \label{item:LiftingCondition}
        $(\sigma')``\bar{G}\subseteq G$.
		      
                                 
                                                                         
	\end{enumerate}
<!-- end defn-7 -->
<!-- defn-8: body_md -->
\label{def:ForcingClasses}
We write $\ensuremath{\mathsf{SSP}}$ for the class of all forcing notions that preserve stationary subsets of $\omega_1$,  and $\ensuremath{\infty\text{-}\mathsf{SC}}\xspace$ for the class of all $\infty$-subcomplete forcing notions. We write $\infty$-\ensuremath{\mathsf{SCFA}}\xspace for $\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}_{\omega_1}(\infty$-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SCFA}}\xspace)$ and \ensuremath{\mathsf{SCFA}}\xspace for the forcing axiom for the original class of subcomplete forcing.
<!-- end defn-8 -->
<!-- defn-9: body_md -->
\label{def:ProjectiveStationarity}
Let $A$ be a set with $\omega_1\subseteq A$. Then,
a set $X\subseteq[A]^\omega$ is \emph{projective stationary in $[A]^\omega$} if for every stationary $T\subseteq\omega_1$, the set
\[X_T=\{x\in X\;|\; x\cap\omega_1\in T\}\]
is stationary in $[A]^\omega$.
<!-- end defn-9 -->
<!-- fact-4: body_md -->
\label{fact:Feng-Jech}
A set $X\subseteq[H_\kappa]^\omega$ is projective stationary iff ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ is stationary set preserving. Thus, \ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace is equivalent to saying that for every regular cardinal $\kappa>\omega_1$ and for every set $X$ which is projective stationary in $[H_\kappa]^\omega$, there is an increasing continuous $\in$-chain of length $\omega_1$ through $X$.
<!-- end fact-4 -->
<!-- defn-10: body_md -->
\label{def:SpreadOut}
Let $B$ be a set (usually of the form $B=H_\kappa$, for some regular uncountable cardinal $\kappa$). A set $X\subseteq[B]^\omega$ with $\bigcup X=B$ is \emph{spread out} (in $B$) if for every sufficiently large cardinal $\theta$ with $X\in H_\theta$, whenever $\tau$, $A$, $M$ and $a$ are such that $H_\theta\subseteq \mathcal{L}_\tau^A=\mathcal{N}\models\ensuremath{{\ensuremath{\mathsf{ZFC}}\xspace}^-}\xspace$, $\theta<\tau$, $X,a,\theta\in M \in [L_\tau [A]]^\omega$, $\mathcal{N}|M\prec \mathcal{N}$, and $\mathcal{N}|M$ is full, then there are an $M' \in [L_\tau [A]]^\omega$ such that $\mathcal{N}|M'\prec \mathcal{N}$ and $M'\cap B\in X$, and an isomorphism $\pi:\mathcal{N}|M\longrightarrow \mathcal{N}|M'$ such that $\pi(a)=a$.

If $\kappa$ is a cardinal and $X$ satisfies this property with the additional requirement that $\pi{\restriction}\kappa$ is the identity, then $X$ is \emph{spread out above $\kappa$}.
<!-- end defn-10 -->
<!-- fact-5: body_md -->
For an uncountable regular cardinal $\kappa$, a set $X\subseteq[H_\kappa]^\omega$ is spread out in $H_\kappa$ iff ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ is $\infty$-subcomplete. Thus, the principle $\ensuremath{\infty\text{-}\mathsf{SC}}\xspace$-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace (\kappa)$ holds iff every spread out subset of $[H_\kappa]^\omega$ contains an increasing continuous $\in$-chain of length $\omega_1$.
<!-- end fact-5 -->
<!-- fact-6: body_md -->
\label{fact:CHneededForLocalCorrespondence}
The principle $\infty$-\ensuremath{\mathsf{SC}}\xspace-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace(\kappa)$ holds for regular uncountable $\kappa\le 2^\omega$.
<!-- end fact-6 -->
<!-- obs-1: body_md -->
Let $\omega_1 \subseteq B_0 \subseteq A\subseteq B_1$, and let $X\subseteq [A]^\omega$ be spread out in $A$. Then $X\mathbin{\downarrow}[B_0]^\omega$ and $X\mathbin{\uparrow}[B_1]^\omega$ are spread out in $B_0$ and $B_1$, respectively.
<!-- end obs-1 -->
<!-- lem-1: body_md -->
\label{lem:OneStep}
Let $\kappa>2^\omega$ be a regular cardinal, $\theta>2^{2^\kappa}$ regular, $\mathcal{N}$ a transitive model of $\ensuremath{{\ensuremath{\mathsf{ZFC}}\xspace}^-}\xspace$ (in a finite language) with a definable well-ordering of its universe and $H_\theta\subseteq \mathcal{N}$, $\sigma:\bar{\mathcal{N}}\prec \mathcal{N}$, where $\bar{\mathcal{N}}$ is countable, transitive and full, with $\kappa\in {\rm ran} (\sigma)$. Let $\eta\in\kappa\cap {\rm ran} (\sigma)$ be such that $\eta^\omega<\kappa$. Let $\bar{\kappa},\bar{\eta}$ be the preimages of $\kappa,\eta$ under $\sigma$, respectively. Let $\bar{a}$ be some element of $\bar{\mathcal{N}}$.
                                                                                            

Then there is an $\omega$-club of ordinals $\alpha<\kappa$ for which there is an embedding $\sigma':\bar{\mathcal{N}}\prec \mathcal{N}$ with the following properties:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\alph*)}]
  \item
  \label{item:MoveBasicParametersTheSameWay}
                                                                                                                                           
  Letting $p=\{\bar{a},\bar{\kappa},\bar{\eta}\}$, we have that $\sigma{\restriction} p=\sigma'{\restriction} p$.
        
                                
                                                               
  \item
  \label{item:SameBehaviorBelow}
  $\sigma{\restriction}\bar{\eta}=\sigma'{\restriction}\bar{\eta}$.
  \item
  \label{item:CorrectSupremum}
  $\sup\sigma'``{\bar{\kappa}}=\alpha$.
        
                                    
                                                              
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lem-1 -->
<!-- defn-11: body_md -->
\label{def:MagicalSets}
Let $\omega_1\le\kappa<\lambda$ be regular cardinals. Let ${\vec{S}}$, ${\vec{T}}$ be $\lambda$-sequences such that for $\alpha<\lambda$, $S_\alpha\subseteq S^\lambda_\omega$ is stationary in $\lambda$ and $T_\alpha\subseteq S^\kappa_\omega$ is stationary in $\kappa$, and such that for $\alpha<\beta<\lambda$, $T_\alpha\cap T_\beta$ is not stationary in $\kappa$.

Then $X_{{\vec{S}},{\vec{T}}}$ is the set consisting of all $M\in [H_\lambda]^\omega$ such that either there is no $\alpha\in M\cap\lambda$ with $\sup(M\cap\kappa)\in T_\alpha$, in which case we require that $\sup(M\cap\lambda)\in S_0$, or there is a unique such $\alpha$, and then $\sup(M\cap\lambda)\in S_\alpha$.
<!-- end defn-11 -->
<!-- lem-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:MagicalSetsSpreadOutAboveKappa}
Let $\omega_1\le\kappa<\lambda$ be regular cardinals, and suppose that $\kappa^\omega<\lambda$. Let ${\vec{S}}$, ${\vec{T}}$ be $\lambda$-sequences as in Definition \ref{def:MagicalSets}. Then $X=X_{{\vec{S}},{\vec{T}}}$ is spread out above $\kappa$.
<!-- end lem-2 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
In the setting of Definition \ref{def:SpreadOut}, let $\mathcal{N}|M\prec \mathcal{N}=\mathcal{L}_\tau^A\supseteq H_\theta$, with ${\vec{S}}, {\vec{T}}, X\in M$, and fix some $a\in M$. Let $\gamma=\sup(M\cap\kappa)$.

\medskip
\noindent\emph{Case 1:} There is an $\alpha\in M\cap\lambda$ such that $\gamma\in T_\alpha$. 

This $\alpha$ is then unique, because if $\beta\in M\cap\lambda$ is different from $\alpha$, then there is a $C\in M$ which is a club subset of $\kappa$ such that $T_\alpha\cap T_\beta\cap C=\emptyset$ (we use here that ${\vec{T}}\in M$, so $T_\alpha$ and $T_\beta$ are also in $M$). Clearly, $\gamma\in C$, so it cannot be that $\gamma\in T_\beta$. 

By Lemma \ref{lem:OneStep}, since $\kappa^\omega<\lambda$, there is an $M'$ such that there is an isomorphism $\pi:\mathcal{N}|M\longrightarrow \mathcal{N}|M'$ that fixes $\alpha,{\vec{S}},{\vec{T}},X$ and $a$, so that $M\cap\kappa=M'\cap\kappa$ (this is the extra condition for being spread out above $\kappa$), and such that $\sup(M'\cap\lambda)\in S_\alpha$. Then $\gamma=\sup(M'\cap\kappa)$, and by the same argument as above, $\alpha$ is the unique $\beta\in M'\cap\lambda$ such that $\gamma\in T_\beta$. Hence, $M'\cap H_\lambda\in X$.

\medskip
\noindent\emph{Case 2:} There is no $\alpha\in M\cap\lambda$ such that $\gamma\in T_\alpha$. 

Then, again by Lemma \ref{lem:OneStep}, there is an $M'\prec \mathcal{N}$ such that $\mathcal{N}|M\cong \mathcal{N}|M'$, the isomorphism fixes any desired parameter, $M\cap\kappa=M'\cap\kappa$, and $\sup(M'\cap\lambda)\in S_0$. It follows that $M'\cap H_\lambda\in X$, unless there is an $\alpha\in M'\cap\lambda$ such that $\gamma\in T_\alpha$ (and $\sup(M'\cap\lambda)\notin S_\alpha$). If so, we can run the argument from case 1 to find an $M''$ isomorphic to $M'$ with $M''\cap\kappa=M'\cap\kappa$ and $M''\cap H_\lambda\in X$. 

\medskip
Thus, in both cases, we found an isomorphic copy of $M$ whose intersection with $H_\lambda$ is in $X$, such that the isomorphism is the identity below $\kappa$, verifying that $X$ is spread out above $\kappa$.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- lem-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:StronglyExactSimultaneousRP}
Let $\lambda > \omega_1$ be a regular cardinal and ${\vec{S}}$ be a $\lambda$-sequence such that for $\alpha < \lambda$, $S_\alpha \subseteq S^\lambda_\omega$ is stationary in $\lambda$. Suppose ${\langleM_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi < \omega_1\rangle}$ is an increasing continuous $\in$-chain of countable sets with $ {\langle M_\xi,{\in} \rangle}  \prec  {\langle H_\lambda,{\in} \rangle} $, and let $R = \bigcup_{\xi<\omega_1} M_\xi \cap \lambda$ and $\rho = \sup R$. Suppose there exists a sequence ${\langleT_\alpha\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha \in R\rangle}$ of stationary subsets of $\omega_1$ with the following properties.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\arabic*)}]
\item For any $\xi < \omega_1$ and any $\alpha \in R$, if $\alpha \in M_\xi \cap \lambda$ and $M_\xi \cap \omega_1 \in T_\alpha$, then $\sup ( M_\xi \cap \lambda ) \in S_\alpha$.
\item For any $\xi < \omega_1$ there is $\alpha \in M_\xi \cap \lambda$ with $M_\xi \cap \omega_1 \in T_\alpha$.
\end{enumerate}
Then $R$ contains a club in $\rho$, and $\rho$ is a strongly exact simultaneous reflection point of ${\vec{S}} | R$.
<!-- end lem-3 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
Let $\rho_\xi = \sup ( M_\xi \cap \lambda )$. Then $\{ \rho_\xi \;|\; \xi < \omega_1 \}$ is club in $\rho$ and is included in $R$. Below we prove the latter statement.

Let $C = \{ \xi < \omega_1 \;|\; M_\xi \cap \omega_1 = \xi \}$. Then $C$ is club in $\omega_1$. For each $\alpha \in R$, by shrinking $T_\alpha$ if necessary, we may assume that $T_\alpha \subseteq C$ and $\alpha \in M_\xi \cap \lambda$ for all $\xi \in T_\alpha$. Then by (1) if $\xi \in T_\alpha$, then $\rho_\xi \in S_\alpha$. Then $S_\alpha \cap \rho$ is stationary for each $\alpha \in R$ since $T_\alpha$ is stationary in $\omega_1$, and the map sending $\xi$ to $\rho_\xi$ is increasing continuous and cofinal in $\rho$. Thus $\rho$ is a simultaneous reflection point of ${\vec{S}} |R$.

To see that $\rho$ is a strongly exact reflection point of ${\vec{S}} |R$, take an arbitrary stationary $U \subseteq \rho$. We find $\alpha \in R$ with $S_\alpha \cap U$ stationary. Since $U$ is stationary, we have that $X = \{ M_\xi \mid \xi < \omega_1 , \ \rho_\xi \in U \}$ is stationary in $[ M_{\omega_1} ]^\omega$, where $M_{\omega_1} = \bigcup_{\xi < \omega_1} M_\xi$. By (2), for each $M \in X$ take $\alpha_M \in M \cap \lambda$ with $M \cap \omega_1 \in T_{\alpha_M}$. By the version of Fodor's theorem for stationary subsets of $[ M_{\omega_1} ]^\omega$ (\cite[Thm.~8.24]{ST3}), we can take $\alpha \in R$ and a stationary $Y \subseteq X$ such that $\alpha_M = \alpha$ for all $M \in Y$. Now let $W = \{ \sup ( M \cap \lambda ) \;|\; M \in Y \}$. Then $W$ is stationary in $\rho$, $W \subseteq U$, and $W \subseteq S_\alpha$ by (1). Thus $S_\alpha \cap U$ is stationary.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- thm-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:GettingeDSR}
Let $\lambda>2^\omega$ be a regular cardinal such that
$\ensuremath{\infty\text{-}\mathsf{SC}}\xspace$-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace(\lambda)$ holds and $\mathsf{NS}_{\omega_1}$ is not $\lambda$-saturated. Then strongly exact $\mathsf{DSR}(\omega_1,S^\lambda_\omega)$ holds.
<!-- end thm-1 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
Let ${\vec{S}} = {\langleS_{\alpha,\xi}\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha<\lambda,\ \xi<\omega_1\rangle}$ be a sequence of stationary subsets of $S^\lambda_\omega$. We find a $\rho < \lambda$ and an $R \subseteq \rho$ such that $R$ contains a club in $\rho$, and $\rho$ is a strongly exact simultaneous reflection point of ${\vec{S}} |R$.

Since $\mathsf{NS}_{\omega_1}$ is not $\lambda$-saturated, we may fix a sequence ${\langleT_\alpha\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha<\lambda\rangle}$ of pairwise almost disjoint stationary subsets of $\omega_1$, in the sense that if $\alpha<\beta<\lambda$, then $T_\alpha\cap T_\beta$ is nonstationary.
Fix a bijection $f:\lambda\longrightarrow\lambda\times\omega_1$. Let $S_\alpha ' =S_{f(\alpha)}$ for $\alpha < \lambda$, and
let ${\vec{S}} ' ={\langleS_\alpha '\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha < \lambda\rangle}$.

Let $X=X_{{\vec{S}} ',{\vec{T}}}$ be the set from Definition \ref{def:MagicalSets}. By Lemma \ref{lem:MagicalSetsSpreadOutAboveKappa}, $X$ is spread out (above $\omega_1$, but every spread out set is). Let $Z$ be the set of all $M \in [H_\lambda]^\omega$ with $ {\langle M,{\in},f{\restriction} M \rangle} \prec {\langle H_\lambda,{\in},f \rangle} $.
Then $Z$ is club, and so, $X\cap Z$ is also spread out, by \cite[Obs.~2.29]{Fuchs:CanonicalFragmentsOfSRP}. By $\ensuremath{\infty\text{-}\mathsf{SC}}\xspace$-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace(\lambda)$, let ${\langleM_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi< \omega_1\rangle}$ be an increasing continuous $\in$-chanin through $X\cap Z$. Let $M_{\omega_1} = \bigcup_{\xi < \omega_1} M_\xi$, $R = M_{\omega_1} \cap \lambda$ and $\rho = \sup R$.

Note that the set
\[ T_0 ' = T_0 \cup \{ \xi < \omega_1 \;|\; \text{there is no $\alpha \in M_\xi \cap \lambda$ with $M_\xi \cap \omega_1 \in T_\alpha$} \} \]
together with ${\langleT_\alpha\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha \in R \setminus \{ 0 \}\rangle}$ satisfies the assumption of Lemma \ref{lem:StronglyExactSimultaneousRP}.
Thus $R$ contains a club in $\rho$, and $\rho$ is a strongly exact simultaneous reflection point of ${\vec{S}} ' |R$.
But ${\vec{S}} '|R = {\vec{S}} |R$ since $M_{\omega_1}$ is closed under $f$ and $f^{-1}$. Thus $\rho$ and $R$ are as desired.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- obs-2: body_md -->
\label{obs:Woodin}
$\ensuremath{\infty\text{-}\mathsf{SC}}\xspace$-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace$ $+$ $\ensuremath{\mathsf{CH}}\xspace$ implies that the nonstationary ideal on $\omega_1$ is not $\omega_2$-saturated.
<!-- end obs-2 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
Assume $\ensuremath{\infty\text{-}\mathsf{SC}}\xspace$-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace + \ensuremath{\mathsf{CH}}\xspace$. 

By \cite[Thm.~3.19]{Fuchs:CanonicalFragmentsOfSRP}, a principle called the strong Friedman property holds at every regular $\kappa>2^\omega=\omega_1$. By \cite[Obs.~3.14]{Fuchs:CanonicalFragmentsOfSRP}, this property implies exact $\ensuremath{\mathsf{Refl}}(\omega_1,S^\kappa_\omega)$ for every regular $\kappa>2^\omega$. And by a folklore result, already $\ensuremath{\mathsf{Refl}}(2,S^\kappa_\omega)$ implies the failure of the principle $\Box(\kappa)$; for a proof we refer to \cite[Thm.~2.1]{Hayut-LambieHanson:SimultaneousReflectionAndSquare}.

Thus, under our assumption, $\square(\kappa)$ fails, for every regular cardinal $\kappa\ge\omega_2$. In particular, for every regular cardinal $\kappa\geq\omega_2$, both $\square(\kappa)$ and $\square_\kappa$ fail (as $\square_\kappa$ implies $\square(\kappa^+)$). This implies, by \cite[Proposition 2.2]{Schimmerling:CoherentSequencesAndThreads}, that $A^\#$ exists, for every $A\subseteq\kappa^+$. So, by choosing $\kappa\ge 2^{\omega_1}$, ${\mathcal{P}}(\omega_1)^\#$ exists. 

If the nonstationary ideal on $\omega_1$ were saturated, then this would imply by Woodin \cite[Theorem 3.17]{Woodin:ADforcingAxiomsNonstationaryIdeal} that $\mathord{\vtop{\ialign{##\crcr
$\hfil\displaystyle{\delta}\hfil$\crcr\noalign{\kern1.5pt\nointerlineskip}
$\hfil\tilde{}\hfil$\crcr\noalign{\kern1.5pt}}}}{}^1_2=\omega_2$. But this contradicts \ensuremath{\mathsf{CH}}\xspace, since if $\mathord{\vtop{\ialign{##\crcr
$\hfil\displaystyle{\delta}\hfil$\crcr\noalign{\kern1.5pt\nointerlineskip}
$\hfil\tilde{}\hfil$\crcr\noalign{\kern1.5pt}}}}{}^1_2=\omega_2$, then there are at least $\omega_2$ $\mathord{\vtop{\ialign{##\crcr
$\hfil\displaystyle{\Sigma}\hfil$\crcr\noalign{\kern1.5pt\nointerlineskip}
$\hfil\tilde{}\hfil$\crcr\noalign{\kern1.5pt}}}}{}^1_2$-definable prewellorderings of ${\mathord{\mathbb{R}}}$, so since there are only continuum many (boldface) projective sets of reals, $\omega_2\le 2^\omega$.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- cor-1: body_md -->
Assume $\ensuremath{\infty\text{-}\mathsf{SC}}\xspace$-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace(\omega_2)$ $+$ \ensuremath{\mathsf{CH}}\xspace $+$ $\mathsf{NS}_{\omega_1}$ is not $\omega_2$-saturated. Then strongly exact $\mathsf{DSR}(\omega_1,S^{\omega_2}_\omega)$ holds, and hence, 
$\ensuremath{\mathsf{OSR}}\xspace_{\omega_2}$ holds.
<!-- end cor-1 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
By Theorem \ref{thm:GettingeDSR}, strongly exact $\mathsf{DSR}(\omega_1,S^{\omega_2}_\omega)$ holds, and by \cite[Lemma 2.4]{Fuchs-LambieHanson:SeparatingDSR}, this implies $\ensuremath{\mathsf{OSR}}\xspace_{\omega_2}$.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- thm-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:SC-SRP+CHimpliesOSR}
$\ensuremath{\infty\text{-}\mathsf{SC}}\xspace$-\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace $+$ $\ensuremath{\mathsf{CH}}\xspace$ implies strongly exact $\mathsf{DSR}(\omega_1,S^{\omega_2}_\omega)$ and hence strongly exact $\ensuremath{\mathsf{OSR}}\xspace_{\omega_2}$.
<!-- end thm-2 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
By Observation \ref{obs:Woodin}, $\mathsf{NS}_{\omega_1}$ is not $\omega_2$-saturated, so the previous corollary applies.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
Under $\ensuremath{\infty\text{-}\mathsf{SC}}\xspace$-\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace $+$ \ensuremath{\mathsf{CH}}\xspace, exact $\ensuremath{\mathsf{Refl}}(\omega_1,S^{\omega_2}_\omega)$ holds, which implies, by Fact \ref{fact:PointOfExactness}, that $\omega_2^{\omega_1}=\omega_2$. Thus, $\mathsf{NS}_{\omega_1}$ is $\omega_3$-saturated.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- question-1: body_md -->
Is the theory \[\ensuremath{\mathsf{ZFC}}\xspace + \ensuremath{\infty\text{-}\mathsf{SC}}\xspace\text{-}\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace((2^\omega)^+)+\neg\ensuremath{\mathsf{CH}}\xspace + \text{``$\mathsf{NS}_{\omega_1}$ is not $(2^\omega)^+$-saturated''}\] consistent?
<!-- end question-1 -->
<!-- defn-12: body_md -->
\label{def:GammaPrime}
For a forcing class $\Gamma$, let
\[\Gamma^-=\Gamma\cap\{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X\;|\;\text{there is a regular}\ \lambda>\omega_1\ \text{such that}\ X\subseteq[H_\lambda]^\omega\ \text{is stationary}\}.\]
<!-- end defn-12 -->
<!-- defn-13: body_md -->
\label{def:FAversionOfSRP}
Let $\Gamma$ be a forcing class. Then $\Gamma$-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}$ denotes $\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}_{\omega_1}(\Gamma^-)$. In the case where $\Gamma=\ensuremath{\mathsf{SSP}}$, we abbreviate this by $\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}$.
<!-- end defn-13 -->
<!-- defn-14: body_md -->
Let $\Gamma$ be a forcing class. Then $\Gamma$-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^{++}$ stands for the forcing axiom  $\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}_{\omega_1}^{++}(\Gamma^-)$. As expected, $\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^{++}$ denotes $\ensuremath{\mathsf{SSP}}$-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^{++}$.
<!-- end defn-14 -->
<!-- lem-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:SRP++ImpliesExactDSR}
$\ensuremath{\infty\text{-}\mathsf{SC}}\xspace$-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^{++}+\ensuremath{\mathsf{CH}}\xspace$ implies strongly exact $\mathsf{DSR}(\omega_1,S^\lambda_\omega)$, for every regular cardinal $\lambda > \omega_1$.
<!-- end lem-4 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
Let a sequence ${\vec{S}}={\langleS_{\alpha,\xi}\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha < \lambda,\ \xi < \omega_1\rangle}$ of stationary subsets of $S^\lambda_\omega$ be given. We find $\rho < \lambda$ and $R \subseteq \rho$ witnessing strongly exact $\mathsf{DSR} ( \omega_1 , S^\lambda_\omega )$ for ${\vec{S}}$.

Fix a bijection $f:\lambda\longrightarrow\lambda\times\omega_1$. Let $S_\alpha ' =S_{f(\alpha)}$ for $\alpha < \lambda$, and
let ${\vec{S}} ' ={\langleS_\alpha '\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha < \lambda\rangle}$. Let $X$ be the set of all $M\in [H_\lambda]^\omega$ such that $ {\langle M,{\in},f{\restriction} M \rangle} \prec {\langle H_\lambda,{\in},f \rangle} $ and there is an $\alpha \in M \cap \lambda$ with $\sup ( M \cap \lambda ) \in S_\alpha '$. $X$ is spread out by Lemma \ref{lem:OneStep} and \cite[Obs.~2.29]{Fuchs:CanonicalFragmentsOfSRP}. Let ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}={\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$. If $G$ is ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$-generic, then $\bigcup G$ is a sequence ${\langleM_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi<\omega_1\rangle}$. Let $\dot{M}_\xi$ be a name for the $\xi$-th element of $\bigcup\dot{G}$, where $\dot{G}$ is the canonical ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$-name for the generic filter. Further, for $\alpha<\lambda$, let $\dot{T}_\alpha$ be a ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$-name such that
\[\Vdash_{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}}\dot{T}_\alpha =\{ \dot{M}_\xi \cap \omega_1 \;|\; \check{\xi} < \omega_1\ \land\ \check{\alpha} \in\dot{M}_\xi\ \land\ \sup(\dot{M}_\xi\cap\check{\lambda})\in\check{S}_\alpha '\}.\]
\begin{NumberedClaim}
\label{NC:BxiStationary}
For any $\alpha<\lambda$, $\Vdash_{\mathord{\mathbb P}}\text{``$\dot{T}_\alpha$ is stationary in $\omega_1$.''}$	
\end{NumberedClaim}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \ref{NC:BxiStationary}.]
Fix $\alpha < \lambda$. Let $\dot{C}$ be a ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$-name and $p\in{\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ a condition that forces that $\dot{C}$ is a club in $\omega_1$. We find $q\leq p$ and $\zeta < \omega_1$ such that $q\Vdash_{\mathord{\mathbb P}}\text{``$\check{\zeta}\in \dot{T}_\alpha \cap \dot{C}$.''}$

Let $\theta$ be a sufficiently large regular cardinal and $\mathcal{N} =  {\langle H_\theta,\in \rangle} $.
Take $M \in [H_\theta]^\omega$ with $\dot{C},X,{\vec{S}} ' ,p,\alpha\in M$, $\mathcal{N}|M\prec {\langle H_\theta,{\in} \rangle} $ and $\sup (M\cap\lambda) \in S_\alpha '$, and let $\zeta = M \cap \omega_1$. Note that $M\cap H_\lambda \in X$. Take an enumeration ${\langleD_n\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{n<\omega\rangle}$ of all dense subsets of ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ in $M$ and a descending sequence ${\langlep_n\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{n<\omega\rangle}$ in ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}\cap M$ below $p$ such that $p_n \in D_n$. Let $q' = \bigcup_{n<\omega} p_n$. Since for every $\xi<\omega_1$, the set of conditions in ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ of length at least $\xi$ is dense, the length of $q'$ is $\zeta$. Since for every $a\in H_\lambda$, the set of conditions $r$ such that $a\in\bigcup {\rm ran} (r)$ is dense, $\bigcup {\rm ran} (q')=M \cap H_\lambda \in X$. Hence, $q=q'{{}^\frown} {\langle M\cap H_\lambda \rangle} $ is a condition in ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ that extends $p$.

Since $\alpha\in M$, $\sup (M\cap\lambda)\in S_\alpha '$, and $q\Vdash_{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}} \text{``$\dot{M}_\zeta = M \cap H_\lambda$''}$, we have that $q$ forces $\zeta = M \cap \omega_1 \in \dot{T}_\alpha$. Since for every $\xi<\omega_1$, the set of conditions $r$ such that there is $\eta\ge\xi$ with $r\Vdash_{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}}\text{``$\check{\eta}\in\dot{C}$''}$ is dense, it follows that $q$ forces that $\check{\zeta}$ is a limit point of $\dot{C}$, and hence that $\check{\zeta}\in\dot{C}$. Thus $q$ and $\zeta$ are as desired. \hspace*\fill$\qedhere_{\ref{NC:BxiStationary}}$\end{proof}

Let $\theta$ be a sufficiently large regular cardinal and $\mathcal{N}= {\langle H_\theta,{\in} \rangle} $.
By \ensuremath{\infty\text{-}\mathsf{SC}}\xspace-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^{++}$, using a folklore characterization as given by Jensen \cite[\S1, Lemma 2]{Jensen:FAandCH}, we can now take $\sigma:\bar{\mathcal{N}}\prec \mathcal{N}$ with $\bar{\mathcal{N}}$ transitive and $\omega_1\cup\{{\vec{S}},X,{\mathord{\mathbb P}}\}\subseteq {\rm ran} (\sigma)$, and, denoting preimages under $\sigma$ by adding bars, can take a ${\bar{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}}}$-generic filter ${\bar{G}}$ over $\bar{\mathcal{N}}$ such that for every $\alpha<{\bar{\lambda}}$, ${\bar{T}}_\alpha=\dot{{\bar{T}}}_\alpha^{{\bar{G}}}$ is stationary in $\omega_1$, where ${\langle\dot{{\bar{T}}}_\alpha\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha < {\bar{\lambda}}\rangle} = \sigma^{-1} ( {\langle\dot{T}_\alpha\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha < \lambda\rangle} )$. Let $R = ( \sigma``\bar{\mathcal{N}} ) \cap \lambda$ and $\rho = \sup R$. We show that $R$ contains a club in $\rho$, and $\rho$ is a strongly exact simultaneous reflection point of ${\vec{S}} | R$.

Let ${\langle{\bar{M}}_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi<\omega_1\rangle}=\bigcup{\bar{G}}$. So ${\langle{\bar{M}}_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi<\omega_1\rangle}$ is an increasing continuous $\in$-chain through ${\bar{X}}$, and $\bigcup_{\xi<\omega_1} {\bar{M}}_\xi = H_{\bar{\lambda}}^{\bar{\mathcal{N}}}$. Note that, by the definition of $\dot{T}_\alpha$, for any $\xi < \omega_1$ and any $\alpha < {\bar{\lambda}}$, if ${\bar{M}}_\xi \cap \omega_1 \in {\bar{T}}_\alpha$, then $\sup ( {\bar{M}}_\xi \cap {\bar{\lambda}} ) \in {\bar{S}}_\alpha '$, where ${\langle{\bar{S}}_\alpha '\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha<{\bar{\lambda}}\rangle} = \sigma^{-1}({\langleS_\alpha '\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha < \lambda\rangle})$. Note also that for any $\xi < \omega_1$ there is $\alpha \in {\bar{M}}_\xi \cap {\bar{\lambda}}$ with ${\bar{M}}_\xi \cap \omega_1 \in {\bar{T}}_\alpha$ since ${\bar{M}}_\xi \in {\bar{X}}$.

Let $M_\xi = \sigma ( {\bar{M}}_\xi )$ for $\xi<\omega_1$. Note that ${\langleM_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi < \omega_1\rangle}$ is an increasing continuous $\in$-chain through $X$, and $\bigcup_{\xi < \omega_1} M_\xi \cap \lambda = R$. For $\alpha \in R$ let $T_\alpha = \sigma ( {\bar{T}}_{{\bar{\alpha}}} ) = {\bar{T}}_{{\bar{\alpha}}}$. Then ${\langleT_\alpha\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha\in R\rangle}$ satisfies the assumption of Lemma \ref{lem:StronglyExactSimultaneousRP} for ${\vec{S}} '$. So $R$ contains a club in $\rho$, and $\rho$ is a strongly exact simultaneous reflection point of ${\vec{S}} ' |R$. Moreover ${\vec{S}} ' |R = {\vec{S}} |R$ since $\bigcup_{\xi < \omega_1} M_\xi$ is closed under $f$ and $f^{-1}$. Thus $\rho$ and $R$ are as desired.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
Fix $\alpha < \lambda$. Let $\dot{C}$ be a ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$-name and $p\in{\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ a condition that forces that $\dot{C}$ is a club in $\omega_1$. We find $q\leq p$ and $\zeta < \omega_1$ such that $q\Vdash_{\mathord{\mathbb P}}\text{``$\check{\zeta}\in \dot{T}_\alpha \cap \dot{C}$.''}$

Let $\theta$ be a sufficiently large regular cardinal and $\mathcal{N} =  {\langle H_\theta,\in \rangle} $.
Take $M \in [H_\theta]^\omega$ with $\dot{C},X,{\vec{S}} ' ,p,\alpha\in M$, $\mathcal{N}|M\prec {\langle H_\theta,{\in} \rangle} $ and $\sup (M\cap\lambda) \in S_\alpha '$, and let $\zeta = M \cap \omega_1$. Note that $M\cap H_\lambda \in X$. Take an enumeration ${\langleD_n\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{n<\omega\rangle}$ of all dense subsets of ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ in $M$ and a descending sequence ${\langlep_n\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{n<\omega\rangle}$ in ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}\cap M$ below $p$ such that $p_n \in D_n$. Let $q' = \bigcup_{n<\omega} p_n$. Since for every $\xi<\omega_1$, the set of conditions in ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ of length at least $\xi$ is dense, the length of $q'$ is $\zeta$. Since for every $a\in H_\lambda$, the set of conditions $r$ such that $a\in\bigcup {\rm ran} (r)$ is dense, $\bigcup {\rm ran} (q')=M \cap H_\lambda \in X$. Hence, $q=q'{{}^\frown} {\langle M\cap H_\lambda \rangle} $ is a condition in ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ that extends $p$.

Since $\alpha\in M$, $\sup (M\cap\lambda)\in S_\alpha '$, and $q\Vdash_{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}} \text{``$\dot{M}_\zeta = M \cap H_\lambda$''}$, we have that $q$ forces $\zeta = M \cap \omega_1 \in \dot{T}_\alpha$. Since for every $\xi<\omega_1$, the set of conditions $r$ such that there is $\eta\ge\xi$ with $r\Vdash_{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}}\text{``$\check{\eta}\in\dot{C}$''}$ is dense, it follows that $q$ forces that $\check{\zeta}$ is a limit point of $\dot{C}$, and hence that $\check{\zeta}\in\dot{C}$. Thus $q$ and $\zeta$ are as desired. \hspace*\fill$\qedhere_{\ref{NC:BxiStationary}}$
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- defn-15: body_md -->
Let $\Gamma$ be a forcing class. Let $\Gamma^*$ be the class of all forcing notions in $\Gamma$ that are of the form ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}*\dot{{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}}$, where ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ is trivial forcing or of the form $\mathord{\mathrm{Add}}(\omega_1,\lambda)$, for some cardinal $\lambda$, and $\dot{{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}}$ is a ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$-name for a forcing such that
\[\Vdash_{\mathord{\mathbb P}}\text{``there are a regular $\kappa>\omega_1$ and a stationary $X\subseteq[H_{\kappa}]^\omega$ such that $\dot{{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}}={\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$.''}\]
Here, $\mathord{\mathrm{Add}}(\omega_1,\lambda)$ is the usual forcing to add $\lambda$ many subsets of $\omega_1$ with countable conditions.
We refer to $\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}_{\omega_1}(\Gamma^*)$ as the \emph{enhanced $\Gamma$-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}$.} As before, if $\Gamma=\ensuremath{\mathsf{SSP}}$, we just write \emph{enhanced $\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}$}.
<!-- end defn-15 -->
<!-- thm-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:AddingDiamondGivesDiagonalReflection}
Let $\lambda>\omega_1$ be a regular cardinal.
Then
\[(\text{enhanced $\ensuremath{\infty\text{-}\mathsf{SC}}\xspace$-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}+\diamondsuit)\implies$ strongly exact $\mathsf{DSR}(\omega_1,S^\lambda_\omega)$.}\]
<!-- end thm-3 -->
<!-- head-2: body_md -->
The result is local, and slightly stronger than stated. Namely, the forcing axiom for forcing notions of the form $\mathord{\mathrm{Add}}(\omega_1,\lambda)*\dot{{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}}$, where $\mathord{\mathrm{Add}}(\omega_1,\lambda)$ forces that there is a spread out subset of $[H_\lambda]^\omega$ such that $\dot{{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}}={\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ (i.e., ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X\in\infty$-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SC}}\xspace$), is sufficient. To emphasize, asking that $\mathord{\mathrm{Add}}(\omega_1,\lambda)*\dot{{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}}$ is in $\ensuremath{\infty\text{-}\mathsf{SC}}\xspace$ is weaker than asking that $\mathord{\mathrm{Add}}(\omega_1,\lambda)$ forces that $\dot{{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}}$ is in $\ensuremath{\infty\text{-}\mathsf{SC}}\xspace$, hence this modified forcing axiom follows from the enhanced \ensuremath{\infty\text{-}\mathsf{SC}}\xspace-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}$.
<!-- end head-2 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
Let a sequence ${\vec{S}} = {\langleS_{\alpha,\xi}\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha<\lambda,\ \xi<\omega_1\rangle}$ of stationary subsets of $S^\lambda_\omega$ be given. We find $\rho < \lambda$ and $R \subseteq \rho$ witnessing strongly exact $\mathsf{DSR} ( \omega_1 , S^\lambda_\omega )$ for ${\vec{S}}$.

Fixing a bijection $f:\lambda\longrightarrow\lambda\times\omega_1$, define $S_\alpha ' =S_{f(\alpha)}$, for $\alpha<\lambda$, as in the proof of Theorem \ref{thm:GettingeDSR}. Denote ${\langleS_\alpha '\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha<\lambda\rangle}$ by ${\vec{S}} '$.
Fix a $\diamondsuit$-sequence ${\vec{D}}={\langleD_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi<\omega_1\rangle}$.

Let ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}=\mathord{\mathrm{Add}}(\omega_1,\lambda)$. Then ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ is $\omega_2$-c.c., so it preserves $\lambda$ as a regular cardinal, and it is $\omega_1$-closed, so it preserves $S^\lambda_\omega$, stationary subsets of $S^\lambda_\omega$, and $\vec{D}$ as a $\diamondsuit$-sequence. For $\alpha<\lambda$, let $\dot{A}_\alpha$ be the canonical ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$-name for the $\alpha$-th new subset of $\omega_1$ added by ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$, and let $\dot{T}_\alpha$ be a canonical ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$-name for the set
\[\{\xi<\omega_1\;|\;\dot{A}_\alpha\cap\xi=D_\xi\}.\]
Thus, since $\vec{D}$ remains a $\diamondsuit$-sequence in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace^{\mathord{\mathbb P}}$, it is forced by ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ that the $\dot{T}_\alpha$ are a pairwise almost disjoint sequence of stationary subsets of $\omega_1$.

Let $\dot{X}$ be the canonical ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$-name for the set $X_{{\vec{S}} ',\dot{{\vec{T}}}}$ of Definition \ref{def:MagicalSets}. By Lemma \ref{lem:MagicalSetsSpreadOutAboveKappa}, $\dot{X}$ is spread out in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace^{\mathord{\mathbb P}}$. Also, let $\dot{Z}$ be the canonical ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$-name for the set of all $M \in [H_\lambda]^\omega$ with $ {\langle M,{\in},f{\restriction} M \rangle} \prec {\langle H_\lambda^{\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace^{\mathord{\mathbb P}}},{\in},f \rangle} $.
As before, letting $\dot{X}'$ be the canonical ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$-name for $\dot{X} \cap \dot{Z}$, we have that $\dot{X}'$ is spread out in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace^{\mathord{\mathbb P}}$. Let $\dot{{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}}$ be the canonical ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$-name for the forcing ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}'}$.

Now take a sufficiently large regular cardinal $\theta$, and let $\mathcal{N} =  {\langle H_\theta,{\in} \rangle} $. By our assumption, $\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}_{\omega_1}(\{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}*\dot{{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}}\})$ holds, so, appealing to Jensen \cite[\S1, Lemma 1]{Jensen:FAandCH}, for example, let $\bar{\mathcal{N}}$ be transitive, and let $\sigma:\bar{\mathcal{N}}\prec \mathcal{N}$ be such that $\omega_1\cup\{f,\vec{S},\vec{D}\}\subseteq {\rm ran} (\sigma)$, and such that, denoting preimages under $\sigma$ by adding bars as before, there is a filter $\bar{G}*\bar{H}$ which is $\bar{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}}*\dot{\bar{{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}}}$-generic over $\bar{\mathcal{N}}$. Let $R = ( \sigma``\bar{\mathcal{N}} ) \cap \lambda$ and $\rho = \sup R$. We show that $\rho$ and $R$ are as desired.

Since $\omega_1\subseteq {\rm ran} (\sigma)$ and $\sigma\upharpoonright\omega_1= {\rm id} $, we have $\sigma^{-1}(\vec{D})=\vec{D}$ is our original $\diamondsuit$-sequence. Denote $\sigma^{-1}({\langle\dot{A}_\alpha\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha<\lambda\rangle})$ by ${\langle\dot{{\bar{A}}}_\alpha\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha<{\bar{\lambda}}\rangle}$,
$\sigma^{-1}({\langle\dot{T}_\alpha\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha<\lambda\rangle})$ by ${\langle\dot{\bar{T}}_\alpha\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha<{\bar{\lambda}}\rangle}$
and let $\bar{A}_\alpha=(\dot{\bar{A}}_\alpha)^{{\bar{G}}}$, as well as $\bar{T}_\alpha=(\dot{\bar{T}}_\alpha)^{{\bar{G}}}$, for $\alpha<{\bar{\lambda}}$. Then, by the choice of these names, $\bar{T}_\alpha=\{\xi<\omega_1\;|\; {\bar{A}}_\alpha\cap\xi=D_\xi\}$ is a stationary subset of $\omega_1$. (This is because $\vec{D}$ is a $\diamondsuit$-sequence not only in $\bar{\mathcal{N}}$, but in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$ - it is to this end that we assumed $\diamondsuit$ to hold.)

Let ${\langle{\bar{M}}_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi<\omega_1\rangle}=\bigcup{\bar{H}}$. So ${\langle{\bar{M}}_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi<\omega_1\rangle}$ is an increasing continuous $\in$-sequence through $\bar{X}'=(\sigma^{-1}(\dot{X}'))^{{\bar{G}}}$. Let $\bar{T}_\alpha ' = \bar{T}_\alpha$ for $\alpha \in {\bar{\lambda}} \setminus \{ 0 \}$, and let
\[{\bar{T}}_0 ' = {\bar{T}}_0 \cup \{ \xi < \omega_1 \;|\; \text{there is no $\alpha \in {\bar{M}}_\xi \cap \lambda$ with ${\bar{M}}_\xi \cap \omega_1 \in {\bar{T}}_\alpha$} \}. \]
For any $\xi < \omega_1$ and any $\alpha < {\bar{\lambda}}$, if $\alpha \in {\bar{M}}_\xi$ and ${\bar{M}}_\xi \cap \omega_1 \in {\bar{T}}_\alpha '$, then $\sup ( {\bar{M}}_\xi \cap {\bar{\lambda}} ) \in {\bar{S}}_\alpha '$, where ${\langle{\bar{S}}_\alpha '\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha<{\bar{\lambda}}\rangle} = \sigma^{-1}({\langleS_\alpha '\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha < \lambda\rangle})$. Also, for any $\xi < \omega_1$ there is $\alpha \in {\bar{M}}_\xi \cap {\bar{\lambda}}$ with ${\bar{M}}_\xi \cap \omega_1 \in {\bar{T}}_\alpha '$.

Let $M_\xi = \sigma ( {\bar{M}}_\xi )$ for $\xi<\omega_1$. Note that ${\langleM_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi < \omega_1\rangle}$ is an increasing continuous $\in$-chain, and $R= \bigcup_{\xi < \omega_1} M_\xi \cap \lambda = \sigma `` {\bar{\lambda}}$. For $\alpha \in R$ let $T_\alpha = \sigma ( {\bar{T}}_{{\bar{\alpha}}} ' ) = {\bar{T}}_{{\bar{\alpha}}}'$. Then ${\langleT_\alpha\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha\in R\rangle}$ satisfies the assumption of Lemma \ref{lem:StronglyExactSimultaneousRP} for ${\vec{S}} '$. Moreover ${\vec{S}} ' |R = {\vec{S}} |R$ since $\bigcup_{\xi < \omega_1} M_\xi$ is closed under $f$ and $f^{-1}$. Then $R$ contains a club in $\rho$, and $\rho$ is a strongly exact simultaneous reflection point of ${\vec{S}} |R$.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- thm-4: body_md -->
\label{thm:AbstractPreservation}
Let $\Gamma$ be a forcing class closed under restrictions\footnote{$\Gamma$ being closed under restrictions means that if $\mathbb{R}\in\Gamma$ and $r\in\mathbb{R}$, then $\mathbb{R}_{{\le}r}$, the restriction of $\mathbb{R}$ to $\{s\;|\; s\le r\}$, is also in $\Gamma$.} and containing all $\omega_2$-directed closed forcing notions, and assume that $\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}_{\omega_1}(\Gamma)$ holds. Let ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ be a forcing notion, $G\subseteq{\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ generic, and in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]$, let ${\mathord{\mathbb Q}}=\dot{{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}}^G$ and $\mathbb{T}=\dot{\mathbb{T}}^G$ be forcing notions such that
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\arabic*)}]
  \item In $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$, there is a dense $\mathbb{U}\subseteq{\mathord{\mathbb P}}*\dot{\mathbb{T}}$ which is $\omega_2$-directed closed. 
  \footnote{The following slightly weaker condition on $\mathbb{U}$ suffices: whenever $D\subseteq\mathbb{U}$ is a directed subset of cardinality less than $\omega_2$, there is a $p\in{\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ such that for every $ {\langle p',\dot{t} \rangle} \in D$, $p\le p'$.}
  \item ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}*(\dot{{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}}\times\dot{\mathbb{T}})\in\Gamma$.\footnote{Here, $\dot{{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}}\times\dot{\mathbb{T}}$ denotes the canonical ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$-name for the product of $\dot{{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}}$ and $\dot{\mathbb{T}}$.}
\end{enumerate}
Then $\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}_{\omega_1}(\{{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}\})$ holds in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]$.
<!-- end thm-4 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
Assume not.

In $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]$, let $\vec{\Delta}=\dot{\Delta}^G={\langle\Delta_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi<\omega_1\rangle}$ be a sequence of dense open subsets of ${\mathord{\mathbb Q}}$ which cannot be met by a filter in ${\mathord{\mathbb Q}}$. Let $p\in G$ force this.

In $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$, let, for $\xi<\omega_1$, $D_\xi\subseteq{\mathord{\mathbb R}}={\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{{\le}p}*(\dot{{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}}\times\dot{\mathbb{T}})$ be defined by
\[D_\xi=\{ {\langle r,\dot{q},\dot{t} \rangle} \;|\; r\in{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{{\le}p},\  {\langle r,\dot{t} \rangle} \in\mathbb{U},\ \text{and}\ r\Vdash_{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}}\dot{q}\in\dot{\Delta}_{\check{\xi}}\}.\]
Then $D_\xi$ is dense in ${\mathord{\mathbb R}}$, and ${\mathord{\mathbb R}}\in\Gamma$, so we may let $F\subseteq{\mathord{\mathbb R}}$ be a $\{D_\xi\;|\;\xi<\omega_1\}$-generic filter, $F\in\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$. It is easy to construct $\bar{F}\subseteq F$ of size $\omega_1$ such that for all $\xi<\omega_1$, $\bar{F}\cap D_\xi\neq\emptyset$, and such that for all $s,t\in\bar{F}$, there is a $u\in\bar{F}$ such that $u\le s,t$. So $\bar{F}$ is a directed subset of $F$ that generates a $\{D_\xi\;|\;\xi<\omega_1\}$-generic filter in $\mathbb{R}$. Let
\[I=\{ {\langle r,\dot{t} \rangle} \;|\;\exists\dot{q}\  {\langle r,\dot{q},\dot{t} \rangle} \in\bar{F}\}.\]
So $I$ is a directed subset of $\mathbb{U}$, of size at most $\omega_1$. Since $\mathbb{U}$ is $\omega_2$-directed closed, or by our slightly weaker assumption on $\mathbb{U}$, there is an $r^*\in{\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ such that for every $ {\langle r,\dot{t} \rangle} \in I$, $r^*\le r$.

Let $\tilde{F}=\{ {\langle \dot{q},r \rangle} \;|\;\exists\dot{t}\  {\langle r,\dot{q},\dot{t} \rangle} \in\bar{F}\}$. So $\tilde{F}$ is a ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$-name for a subset of $\dot{{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}}$.\\

\noindent\emph{Claim:} $r^*\Vdash_{\mathord{\mathbb P}}``\tilde{F}\ \text{generates a $\dot{\Delta}$-generic filter in $\dot{{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}}$.''}$\\

To see this, let $H$ be ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$-generic over $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$ with $r^*\in H$. Let $a,b\in\tilde{F}^H$. Let $ {\langle \dot{q}_0,r_0 \rangle} , {\langle \dot{q}_1,r_1 \rangle} \in\tilde{F}$, where $r_0,r_1\in H$ and $a=\dot{q}_0^H$, $b=\dot{q}_1^H$. Let $\dot{t}_0,\dot{t}_1$ be such that $ {\langle r_0,\dot{q}_0,\dot{t}_0 \rangle} , {\langle r_1,\dot{q}_1,\dot{t}_1 \rangle} \in\bar{F}$. Since $\bar{F}$ is directed, there is a condition $ {\langle r_2,\dot{q}_2,\dot{t}_2 \rangle} \in\bar{F}$, below both $ {\langle r_0,\dot{q}_0,\dot{t}_0 \rangle} $ and $ {\langle r_1,\dot{q}_1,\dot{t}_1 \rangle} $. In particular, $r_2\le r_0,r_1$ and $r_2\Vdash\dot{q}_2\le\dot{q}_0,\dot{q}_1$. Moreover, since $ {\langle r_2,\dot{t}_2 \rangle} \in I$, it follows that $r^*\le r_2$. Since $r^*\in H$, it follows that $r_2\in H$, and we have that $c=\dot{q}_2^H\le\dot{q}_0^H=a$ and $c\le\dot{q}_1^H=b$, and since $ {\langle \dot{q}_2,r_2 \rangle} \in\tilde{F}$, $c\in\tilde{F}^H$. Thus, $\tilde{F}^H$ generates a filter. Moreover, for $\xi<\omega_1$, there is a condition $ {\langle r,\dot{q},\dot{t} \rangle} \in\bar{F}\cap D_\xi$, so $r\Vdash_{\mathord{\mathbb P}}\dot{q}\in\dot{\Delta}_{\check{\xi}}$. Again, since $ {\langle r,\dot{t} \rangle} \in I$ we have that $r^*\le r$, so $r\in H$. Hence, $\dot{q}^H\in\dot{\Delta}^H_\xi$, and as $ {\langle \dot{q},r \rangle} \in\tilde{F}$, $\dot{q}^H\in\tilde{F}^H$. So the filter generated by $\tilde{F}^H$ is $\dot{\Delta}^H$-generic.

Since $r^*\le p$, this contradicts what $p$ forced.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- defn-16: body_md -->
\label{def:KillingStationarySets}
Let $\lambda$ be an uncountable regular cardinal, and let $A\subseteq\lambda$ be unbounded. The forcing notion $\mathbb{T}_S$ consists of all closed, bounded subsets $t$ of $\lambda$ such that $t \cap S = \emptyset$. The ordering is defined by setting, for $t_0, t_1 \in \mathbb{T}_S$, $t_1 \leq_{\mathbb{T}_S} t_0$ if
$t_1$ \emph{end-extends} $t_0$, that is, $t_1\cap( \max t_0 + 1 )=t_0$.
<!-- end defn-16 -->
<!-- lem-5: body_md -->
\label{lem:BasicsOnT_S}
Let $\lambda$ be an uncountable regular cardinal, and let $S\subseteq\lambda$ be such that $\lambda\setminus S$ is unbounded in $\lambda$.                                                                                       
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\arabic*)}]
  \item
  \label{item:AddingAClub}
  Let $G$ be $\mathbb{T}_S$-generic over $V$. Then
  $\bigcup G$ is a club subset of $\lambda$ that is disjoint from $S$.
  \item
  \label{item:TFAE}
  Suppose that $\mathbb{T}_S$ preserves the fact that $\lambda$ has uncountable cofinality (in our applications, $\mathbb{T}_S$ will be $\lambda$-distributive, so this will certainly be the case).
  Then, for a set $T\subseteq\lambda$ such that $T\setminus S\subseteq S^\lambda_\omega$, the following are equivalent:
  \begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\alph*)}]
    \item
    \label{item:T_SpreservesTAlways}
    $\Vdash_{\mathbb{T}_S}$ ``$\check{T}$ is stationary in $\check{\lambda}$.''
    \item
    \label{item:T_SpreservesTSometimes}
    There is a $t\in\mathbb{T}_S$ such that $t\Vdash_{\mathbb{T}_S}$ ``$\check{T}$ is stationary in $\check{\lambda}$.''
    \item
    \label{item:TminusSstationary}
    $T\setminus S$ is stationary.
  \end{enumerate}
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lem-5 -->
<!-- lem-6: body_md -->
\label{lem:WhenT_APreservesGenStationarity}
Let $\lambda>\omega_1$ be regular, $H\supseteq\lambda$ a set, $S\subseteq\lambda$ stationary in $\lambda$ and $X\subseteq[H]^\omega$ stationary in $[H]^\omega$, so that $\lambda\setminus S$ is unbounded in $\lambda$ and $\mathbb{T}_S$ is countably distributive (this is the case, for example, if $S\subseteq S^\lambda_\omega$ and $S^\lambda_\omega \setminus S$ is stationary). Then the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\arabic*)}]
\item
\label{item:SminusAisStationary}
$X\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S,[H]^\omega)$ is stationary.
\item
\label{item:StationarityOfSisPreserved}
$\mathbb{T}_S$ preserves the stationarity of $X$.
\item
\label{item:SomebodyPreservesStationarityOfS}
There is a condition $p\in\mathbb{T}_S$ that forces that $\check{X}$ is stationary.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lem-6 -->
<!-- lem-7: body_md -->
\label{lem:PreservationOfP_SbeingSSP}
Let $\theta\ge\lambda>\omega_1$ be regular cardinals, and let $S\subseteq S^\lambda_\omega$ be stationary. Let
$X$ be a stationary subset of $[H_\theta]^\omega$. Suppose that $\mathbb{T}_S$ is countably distributive. If for every stationary $T\subseteq\omega_1$, $X_T\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S,[H_\theta]^\omega)$ is stationary, then $\mathbb{T}_S\times{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ is stationary set preserving. Here, we use the notation from Definition \ref{def:ProjectiveStationarity}, writing $X_T=\{x\in X\;|\; x\cap\omega_1\in T\}$.
<!-- end lem-7 -->
<!-- thm-5: body_md -->
\label{thm:ALaLarson}
Assume \ensuremath{\mathsf{MM}}\xspace. Let ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\omega_2}$ be the forcing to add a counterexample to $\ensuremath{\mathsf{OSR}}\xspace_{\omega_2}$. Then, in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace^{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\omega_2}}$, $\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}$ holds (while $\ensuremath{\mathsf{OSR}}\xspace_{\omega_2}$ fails).
<!-- end thm-5 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
Let $\vec{S}={\langleS_\alpha\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha<\omega_2\rangle}$ be the generic sequence of stationary subsets of $S^{\omega_2}_\omega$ added by ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\omega_2}$. It does not reflect in the way postulated by $\ensuremath{\mathsf{OSR}}\xspace_{\omega_2}$, and it forms a partition of $S^{\omega_2}_\omega$. The forcing is countably closed, and, letting $\dot{S}_\alpha$ be a canonical ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\omega_2}$-name for $S_\alpha$, the forcing ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\omega_2}*\dot{\mathbb{T}}_{\dot{S}_\alpha}$ has a suborder which is $\omega_2$-directed closed.

Working in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[\vec{S}]$, fix a projective stationary set $X\subseteq[H_\theta]^\omega$, for some regular $\theta\ge\omega_2$. To show that $\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}_{\omega_1}(\{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X\})$ holds, it suffices by Theorem \ref{thm:AbstractPreservation} to show that there is an $\alpha<\omega_2$ such that ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X\times\mathbb{T}_{S_\alpha}$ is stationary set preserving (as this implies that ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\omega_2}*(\dot{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}}_{\dot{X}}\times\dot{\mathbb{T}}_{\dot{S}_\alpha})$ is stationary set preserving, at least below some condition in ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\omega_2}$ which forces this situation). Since we are in the situation of Lemma \ref{lem:PreservationOfP_SbeingSSP}, it suffices to show the existence of an $\alpha<\omega_2$ such that for every stationary $T\subseteq\omega_1$, $X_T\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S_\alpha,[H_\theta]^\omega)$ is stationary.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    
 Supposing there were no such $\alpha$, we could find, for each $\alpha<\omega_2$, a stationary $T_\alpha\subseteq\omega_1$ such that                                                                  
 $X_{T_\alpha}\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S_\alpha,[H_\theta]^\omega)$ is nonstationary.
 Since $\mathsf{NS}_{\omega_1}$ is $\omega_2$-saturated, there are $\alpha_0<\alpha_1<\omega_2$ such that $T=T_{\alpha_0}\cap T_{\alpha_1}$ is stationary. 
 We would then have that the union of $X_{T_{\alpha_0}}\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S_{\alpha_0},[H_\theta]^\omega)$ and $X_{T_{\alpha_1}}\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S_{\alpha_1},[H_\theta]^\omega)$ is nonstationary. But this union contains $X_T$, since if $x\in X_T$, then $x\cap\omega_1$ is in both $T_{\alpha_0}$ and $T_{\alpha_1}$, and $\sup(x\cap\omega_2)$  must avoid at least one of $S_{\alpha_0}$ and $S_{\alpha_1}$. Since $X_T$ is stationary, this is a contradiction.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- obs-3: body_md -->
\label{obs:MediatedProjectiveStationarity}
Let $\mathbb{A}$ be a countably distributive, stationary set preserving forcing, and let $\dot{X}$ be an $\mathbb{A}$-name for a stationary subset of $[H_\theta]^\omega$ (in the sense of the forcing extension, where $\theta>\omega_1$ is a regular cardinal.)                                                                                                                                            
Then $\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}}$ is stationary set preserving iff for every stationary set $T\subseteq\omega_1$,
\[\Vdash_\mathbb{A}\text{``$\dot{X}_{\check{T}}$ is stationary in $\omega_1$.''}\footnote{Here, we use notation introduced in Definition \ref{def:ProjectiveStationarity}.}\]
<!-- end obs-3 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
Fix a stationary set $T\subseteq\omega_1$. Then $\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}}$ preserves the stationarity of $T$ iff whenever $I\subseteq\mathbb{A}$ is generic, ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}^I}$ preserves the stationarity of $T$ over $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[I]$ (since $\mathbb{A}$ is stationary set preserving by assumption). This latter condition is equivalent to saying that in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[I]$, $(\dot{X}^I)_T$ is stationary. Indeed, the equivalence between the projective stationarity of some $X\subseteq[H_\theta]^\omega$ and the preservation of stationary sets by ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ stated in \cite[Fact 2.9]{Fuchs:CanonicalFragmentsOfSRP} holds locally. One direction of this equivalence can be seen by perusing the proof of \cite[Theorem 1.1]{FengJech:ProjectiveStationarityAndSRP}, showing that if $X_T$ is stationary, then ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ preserves the stationarity of $T$. The argument of the converse is sketched after that proof; here are the details: suppose ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ preserves the stationarity of $T$, but assume towards a contradiction that $X_T$ is not stationary. Let $Z\subseteq[H_\theta]^\omega$ be a club disjoint from $X_T$. If ${\langleM_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi<\omega_1\rangle}$ is a ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$-generic sequence, then $\{M_\xi\;|\;\xi<\omega_1\}$ is also club in $[H^\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace_\theta]^\omega$. Note that ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ is countably distributive, so $[H_\theta^\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace]^\omega$ does not change, and $Z$ remains club. So $W=Z\cap\{M_\xi\;|\;\xi<\omega_1\}$ is a club subset of $X$, but disjoint from $X_T$. Hence, for all $M\in W$, $M\cap\omega_1\notin T$. Thus, $\{M\cap\omega_1\;|\; M\in W\}$ is a club subset of $\omega_1$ disjoint from $T$, so the stationarity of $T$ is not preserved by ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$, a contradiction.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- defn-17: body_md -->
\label{def:PotentialStationarity}
Let $\mathbb{A}$ be a forcing notion, $\lambda$ an ordinal, and let $\dot{X}$ be an $\mathbb{A}$-name such that $\Vdash_\mathbb{A}\text{``$\dot{X}$ is a stationary subset of $[\bigcup\dot{X}]^\omega$, and $\check{\lambda}\subseteq\bigcup{\dot{X}}$.''}$
Then we say that $\dot{X}$ is \emph{$\mathbb{A}$-potentially stationary above $\lambda$.}
<!-- end defn-17 -->
<!-- lem-8: body_md -->
\label{lem:PreservationOfPotentialStationarity}
Let $\lambda>\omega_1$ be a regular cardinal, and let $S\subseteq S^\lambda_\omega$ be stationary. Let $\mathbb{A}$ be a countably distributive forcing, and let $\dot{X}$ be an $\mathbb{A}$-name.
Let $\mathbb{T}_S$ be the forcing to kill the stationarity of $S$.                                                                                                         
If the canonical $\mathbb{A}$-name for $\dot{X}\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(\check{S},[\bigcup\dot{X}]^\omega)$ is $\mathbb{A}$-potentially stationary above $\lambda$, then in any forcing extension by $\mathbb{T}_S$,  $\dot{X}$ is $\mathbb{A}$-potentially stationary above $\lambda$.
<!-- end lem-8 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
We have to show that in any $\mathbb{T}_S$-generic forcing extension, $\mathbb{A}$ forces that $\dot{X}$ is stationary and $\check{\lambda}\subseteq\bigcup\dot{X}$. Write $\mathbb{T}$ for the forcing $\mathbb{T}_S$.
Turning the order of forcing around, letting $I\subseteq\mathbb{A}$ be an arbitrary generic filter, and setting $X=\dot{X}^I$ and $H=\bigcup X$, it suffices to show that

\begin{NumberedClaim}
\label{NC:OldT_ApreservesS}
In $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[I]$, $\mathbb{T}$ forces that $\check{X}$ is stationary in $[\check{H}]^\omega$
\end{NumberedClaim}
since we already know by assumption that $\lambda\subseteq H$. Note that $\mathbb{T}$ is the forcing to kill the stationarity of $S$ as defined in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$, which is different from that forcing as defined in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[I]$, that is, $\mathbb{T}=(\mathbb{T}_S)^\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace\neq(\mathbb{T}_S)^{\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[I]}$. So we cannot appeal to Lemma \ref{lem:WhenT_APreservesGenStationarity}.

To prove \ref{NC:OldT_ApreservesS}, assume the contrary. So let $\dot{f}\in\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[I]^{\mathbb{T}}$ be a $\mathbb{T}$-name and $p\in\mathbb{T}$ a condition such that
\[p\Vdash_{\mathbb{T}}^{\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[I]}\text{``$\dot{f}:\check{H}^{{<}\omega}\longrightarrow\check{H}$ and whenever $x\in[\check{H}]^\omega$ is closed under $\dot{f}$, $x\notin\check{X}$.''}\]
By assumption, and arguing in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[I]$, we know that $\tilde{X}=X\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S,[H]^\omega)$ is stationary. Hence, letting $\chi$ be some sufficiently large regular cardinal, so is $\tilde{X}\mathop{\uparrow}[H_\chi^{\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[I]}]^\omega$. So let $M$ be a countable set such that $S,\mathbb{T},p,H,X,\dot{f} \in M$, $ {\langle M,{\in} \rangle}  \prec  {\langle H_\chi^{\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[I]},{\in} \rangle} $, and $x:=M\cap H\in\tilde{X}$. Let $\alpha=\sup(x\cap\lambda)$. Since $\mathbb{A}$ does not add any new $\omega$-sequences of ordinals, the cofinality of $\lambda$ in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[I]$ is still uncountable, so $\alpha<\lambda$, and since $x\notin\mathsf{lifting}(S,[H]^\omega)$, $\alpha\notin S$. Let $g\subseteq\mathbb{T}\cap M$ be $M$-generic for $\mathbb{T}$ with $p\in g$, generated by a decreasing sequence ${\langlet_n\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{n<\omega\rangle}$ of conditions in $\mathbb{T}\cap M$ which is defined so that, once an enumeration ${\langleD_n\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{n<\omega\rangle}$ of the dense subsets of $\mathbb{T}$ which exist in $M$ is fixed, $t_n\in D_n$, for every $n<\omega$. Since $\mathbb{A}$ is countably distributive, the sequence ${\langlet_n\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{n<\omega\rangle}$ is in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$. Note that $\bigcup g=\bigcup_{n<\omega}t_n$ is cofinal in $\alpha$. Hence, as $\alpha\notin S$, the set $q=( \bigcup g )\cup\{\alpha\}$ is a condition in $\mathbb{T}$. Also, by elementarity, $x$ is closed under $\dot{f}^g$, because if $a_0,\ldots,a_{m-1} \in x$, then the set $D$ of conditions which are either incompatible with $p$ or decide the value of $\dot{f}(\check{a}_0,\ldots,\check{a}_{m-1})$ is dense, and it is in $M$, so, since $g$ is generated by $\{t_n\;|\; n<\omega\}$, there is an $n$ such that $r=t_n\in D$. But then $\dot{f}^g(a_0,\ldots,a_{m-1})$ is the unique $b\in H$ such that $r\Vdash_\mathbb{T}\dot{f}(\check{a}_0,\ldots,\check{a}_{m-1})=\check{b}$. Since $r,\dot{f},\vec{a},\mathbb{T},H\in M$, it follows by elementarity that $b$ is definable from these parameters in $M$, so $b\in M$, hence $b\in x$, as $b\in H$.

But, if $J\subseteq\mathbb{T}$ is generic over $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[I]$, with $q\in J$, then $g\subseteq J$, and $\dot{f}^J{\restriction} x^{{<}\omega}=\dot{f}^g$. So $x$ is closed under $\dot{f}^J$, and $x\in\tilde{X}$, contradicting what $p$ forced.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- lem-9: body_md -->
\label{lem:PreservationOfMediatedProjectiveStationarity}
Let $\theta\ge\lambda>\omega_1$ be regular cardinals, and let $S\subseteq S^\lambda_\omega$ be stationary. Let $\mathbb{A}$ be a $\sigma$-closed forcing, and let                                                       
$\dot{X}$ be an $\mathbb{A}$-name such that $\Vdash_\mathbb{A}\bigcup\dot{X}=H_{\check{\theta}}$ (that is, if $I$ is $\mathbb{A}$-generic, then $\bigcup\dot{X}^I=H_\theta^{\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[I]}$). Let $\mathbb{T}_S$ be the forcing to kill the stationarity of $S$, and suppose that $\mathbb{T}_S$ is countably distributive.
If for every stationary $T\subseteq\omega_1$,
\[\Vdash_\mathbb{A}\text{``$\dot{X}_{\check{T}}\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(\check{S},[\bigcup\dot{X}]^\omega)$ is stationary''}\]
then $\mathbb{T}_S\times(\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}})$ is stationary set preserving.
<!-- end lem-9 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
Let $T\subseteq\omega_1$ be stationary, and let $G\subseteq\mathbb{T}_S$ be generic over $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$.
                                                                                                                                                                                           
By our assumption, the canonical $\mathbb{A}$-name for $\dot{X}_T\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(\check{S},[\bigcup{\dot{X}}]^\omega)$ is $\mathbb{A}$-potentially stationary above $\lambda$.

By Lemma \ref{lem:PreservationOfPotentialStationarity}, it follows that in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]$, $\dot{X}_{\check{T}}$ is $\mathbb{A}$-potentially stationary above $\lambda$. Note that this implies that $T$ is stationary in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]$.
In any case, letting $I\subseteq\mathbb{A}$ be generic over $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]$, and setting $X=\dot{X}^I$ and $H=\bigcup X$, we have that in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G][I]$, $X_T$ is stationary in $[H]^\omega$. Note that $H=H_\theta^{\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[I]}$.                                                                                                                                                                   
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           

Working in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G][I]$, we can now apply the argument of the proof of Feng \& Jech \cite[Theorem 1.1]{FengJech:ProjectiveStationarityAndSRP}.
Note that $({\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X)^{\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[I]}=({\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X)^{\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G][I]}$, since  $\mathbb{T}_S\times\mathbb{A}$ is countably distributive, as $\mathbb{A}$ is still $\sigma$-closed in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]$, because $\mathbb{T}_S$ adds no new countable sequences. This implies that $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G][I]=\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[I][G]$ has no new $\omega$-sequence of elements of $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[I]$. So we are justified in just writing ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ for this poset.

Thus, towards a contradiction, suppose $p\in{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ is a condition and $\dot{C}$ is a ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$-name such that
\[p\Vdash_{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X}^{\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G][I]}\text{``$\dot{C}\subseteq\omega_1$ is club and disjoint from $\check{T}$.''}\]
Let $\chi$ be a sufficiently large regular cardinal, and let $M$ be a countable set such that $H,X,T,\dot{C},p \in M$, $ {\langle M,{\in} \rangle}  \prec  {\langle H_\chi^{\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G][I]},{\in} \rangle} $, and $x:=M\cap H\in X_T$ (using the fact that $X_T$ is stationary in $[H]^\omega$ in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G][I]$). So $\zeta=x\cap\omega_1=M\cap\omega_1\in T$.

Let ${\langleD_n\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{n<\omega\rangle}$ enumerate all dense subsets of ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ that are in $M$, and build a decreasing sequence ${\langlep_n\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{n<\omega\rangle}$ in ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X \cap M$ such that $p_0\le p$ and for all $n<\omega$, $p_n\in D_n$. Let $q'=\bigcup_{n<\omega}p_n$.

Since for every $\xi<\omega_1$, the set of conditions in ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ of length at least $\xi$ is dense, the length of $q'$ is $\zeta$. Since for every $a\in H$, the set of conditions $r$ such that $a\in\bigcup {\rm ran} (r)$ is dense, $\bigcup {\rm ran} (q')=x\in X$. Hence, $q=q'{{}^\frown} {\langle x \rangle} $ is a condition in ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ that extends $p$. Since for every $\xi<\omega_1$, the set of conditions $r$ such that there is an $\eta\ge\xi$ with $r\Vdash_{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X}\text{``$\check{\eta}\in\dot{C}$''}$ is dense, it follows that $q$ forces that $\check{\zeta}$ is a limit point of $\dot{C}$, and hence that $\check{\zeta}$ is in $\dot{C}$. So since $\zeta\in T$, $q$ is an extension of $p$ forcing that $\dot{C}\cap\check{T}\neq\emptyset$, contradicting what $p$ forced.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- thm-6: body_md -->
\label{thm:EnhancedSRPDoesntImplyuDSR}
Assume $\ensuremath{\mathsf{MM}}\xspace$, and let $\lambda>\omega_2$ be a regular cardinal. Then there is a cofinality preserving forcing extension $W$ of $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$ in which $\mathsf{uDSR}(1,S^\lambda_\omega)$ fails, yet $\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}_{\omega_1}(\Gamma)$ holds, where, in $W$, $\Gamma$ consists of all stationary set preserving forcing notions of the form
$\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}}$ such that $\mathbb{A}$ is $\sigma$-closed and $\lambda$-c.c., and $\dot{X}$ is an $\mathbb{A}$-name for a stationary subset of $[H_{\check{\mu}}]^\omega$, for some regular $\mu$ (that is, if $I\subseteq\mathbb{A}$ is $\mathbb{A}$-generic over $W$, then $\mu>\omega_1$ is regular in $W[I]$ and $\dot{X}^I$ is a stationary subset of $[H_\mu^{W[I]}]^\omega$).
<!-- end thm-6 -->
<!-- head-3: body_md -->
$\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}_{\omega_1}(\Gamma)$ implies enhanced $\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}$, since every forcing of the form $\mathord{\mathrm{Add}}(\omega_1,\theta)$ is countably closed and $\lambda$-c.c., as $\lambda>2^\omega$. So this theorem in particular separates enhanced $\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}$ from $\mathsf{uDSR}(1,S^\lambda_\omega)$.
<!-- end head-3 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
Let ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ be the $\lambda$-strategically closed forcing used in the proof of \cite[Theorem 3.10]{Fuchs-LambieHanson:SeparatingDSR} to add a counterexample to $\mathsf{uDSR}(1,S^\lambda_\omega)$, by initial segments. It adds a sequence ${\langleS_\alpha\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha<\lambda\rangle}$ of pairwise disjoint stationary subsets of $S^{\lambda}_\omega$ such that $S_\alpha\cap(\alpha+1)=\emptyset$ and such that for every $\rho<\lambda$ of uncountable cofinality, the set $\{\alpha<\rho\;|\; S_\alpha\cap\rho\ \text{is stationary in}\ \rho\}$ is bounded in $\rho$. So this sequence verifies the failure of $\mathsf{uDSR}(1,S^\lambda_\omega)$ in $W=\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[{\vec{S}}]$, and it remains to show that $\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}_{\omega_1}(\Gamma)$ holds in $W$. For $\alpha<\lambda$, we write
\[S_{{\ge}\alpha}=\bigcup_{\alpha\le\beta<\lambda}S_\beta.\]

Work in $W$.
\begin{NumberedClaim}
\label{NC:ThereAlwaysIsAnAlpha}
Let $\dot{X}$ be $\mathbb{A}$-potentially stationary above $\lambda$, and let $\dot{H}$ be a canonical $\mathbb{A}$-name for the union of $\dot{X}$.
 Then
	\[\Vdash_\mathbb{A}\text{``$\exists\alpha<\lambda\quad\dot{X}\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(\check{S}_{{\ge}\alpha},[\dot{H}]^\omega)$ is stationary.''}\]
\end{NumberedClaim}	

\begin{proof}[Proof of \ref{NC:ThereAlwaysIsAnAlpha}. ]
If not, then let $I\subseteq\mathbb{A}$ be generic such that, letting $H=\dot{H}^I$ and $X=\dot{X}^I$, in $W[I]$, for every $\alpha<\lambda$, there is a club $Z_\alpha\subseteq[H]^\omega$ disjoint from $X\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S_{{\ge}\alpha},[H]^\omega)$. That is, for all $\alpha<\lambda$, $X\cap Z_\alpha\subseteq\mathsf{lifting}(S_{{\ge}\alpha},[H]^\omega)$. By the normality of the generalized club filter (see \cite[Lemma 8.23]{ST3}), let $Z\subseteq[H]^\omega$ be club such that for all $x\in Z$, for all $\alpha\in x\cap\lambda$, $x\in Z_\alpha$. Pick $x\in X\cap Z$, and let $\gamma=\sup(x\cap\lambda)<\lambda$. Then, for all $\alpha\in x\cap\lambda$, $x\in Z_\alpha$, and hence, since $x\in X$, $x\in\mathsf{lifting}(S_{{\ge}\alpha},[H]^\omega)$, which means that $\gamma=\sup(x\cap\lambda)\in S_{{\ge}\alpha}$. Since the $S_\alpha$'s are pairwise disjoint, and since $x\cap\lambda$ is unbounded in $\gamma$, it follows that $\gamma\in S_{{\ge}\gamma}$. But, say $\delta\ge\gamma$ is such that $\gamma\in S_\delta$. Then $\gamma\in S_\delta\cap(\delta +1)=\emptyset$, a contradiction. \hspace*{\fill}$\qedhere_{\ref{NC:ThereAlwaysIsAnAlpha}}$
\end{proof}

\begin{NumberedClaim}
\label{NC:AFixedThetaAlwaysWorks}
Let $\dot{X}$, $\dot{H}$ be as in \ref{NC:ThereAlwaysIsAnAlpha}. Then
there is an $\alpha<\lambda$ such that
\[\Vdash_\mathbb{A}\text{``$\dot{X}\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(\check{S}_{{\ge}\alpha},[\dot{H}]^\omega)$ is stationary.''}\]
\end{NumberedClaim}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \ref{NC:AFixedThetaAlwaysWorks}. ]
By \ref{NC:ThereAlwaysIsAnAlpha}, let $\dot{\beta}$ be an $\mathbb{A}$-name such that
\[\Vdash_\mathbb{A}\text{``$\dot{\beta}<\check{\lambda}$ is least such that $\dot{X}\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(\check{S}_{{\ge}\dot{\beta}},[\dot{H}]^\omega)$ is stationary.''}\]
Let $B=\{\beta<\lambda\;|\;\exists p\in\mathbb{A}\quad p\Vdash_\mathbb{A}\dot{\beta}=\check{\beta}\}$, and for each $\beta\in B$, let $p_\beta\Vdash_\mathbb{A}\dot{\beta}=\check{\beta}$. Clearly, if $\beta,\beta ' \in B$ are distinct, then $p_\beta$ and $p_{\beta '}$ are incompatible. So $P=\{p_\beta\;|\;\beta\in P\}$ is an antichain of the same cardinality as $B$. Since $\mathbb{A}$ is $\lambda$-c.c., this cardinality is less than $\lambda$, and since $\lambda$ is regular, $\alpha=\sup B<\lambda$. Then $\alpha$ is as desired, because if $I\subseteq\mathbb{A}$ is generic, then by \ref{NC:ThereAlwaysIsAnAlpha}, there is a minimal $\beta<\lambda$ such that in $W[I]$, $\dot{X}^I\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S_{{\ge}\beta},[\dot{H}^I]^\omega)$ is stationary. But then, $\beta\in B$, and hence, $\beta\le\alpha$. So $S_{{\ge}\alpha}\subseteq S_{{\ge}\beta}$, and hence, in $W[I]$, $\dot{X}^I\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S_{{\ge}\alpha},[\dot{H}^I]^\omega)$ is stationary as well. \hspace*{\fill}$\qedhere_{\ref{NC:AFixedThetaAlwaysWorks}}$
\end{proof}

Now let $\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}}\in\Gamma$, where $\mathbb{A}$ and $\dot{X}$ are as described in the statement of the theorem. Let $\dot{H}$ be an $\mathbb{A}$-name such that $\Vdash_\mathbb{A}\bigcup\dot{X}=\dot{H}$, and let $\mu>\omega_1$ be such that $\Vdash_\mathbb{A}\dot{H}=H_{\check{\mu}}$.

Assume for now that $\mu\ge\lambda$.

\begin{NumberedClaim}
\label{NC:SomeThetaPreservesProjectiveStationarity}
There is an $\alpha<\lambda$ such that for every stationary $T\subseteq\omega_1$, (the canonical name for) $\dot{X}_{\check{T}}\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S_{{\ge}\alpha},[\dot{H}]^\omega)$ is $\mathbb{A}$-potentially stationary.
\end{NumberedClaim}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \ref{NC:SomeThetaPreservesProjectiveStationarity}]
By assumption, $\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}}$ is stationary set preserving, and hence, so is $\mathbb{A}$. By Observation \ref{obs:MediatedProjectiveStationarity}, this means that for every stationary $T\subseteq\omega_1$, $\Vdash_\mathbb{A}\text{``$\dot{X}_{\check{T}}$ is stationary.''}$ Now, fix such a stationary $T$. By \ref{NC:AFixedThetaAlwaysWorks}, there is a $\alpha_T$ such that
\[\Vdash_\mathbb{A}\text{``$\dot{X}_{\check{T}}\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(\check{A}_{{\ge}\alpha_T},[\dot{H}]^\omega)$ is stationary.''}\]
Since $2^{\omega_1}=\omega_2<\lambda$, $\alpha=\sup\{\alpha_T\;|\; T\subseteq\omega_1\ \text{is stationary}\}<\lambda$. It follows as before that $\alpha$ is as wished. \hspace*{\fill}$\qedhere_{\ref{NC:SomeThetaPreservesProjectiveStationarity}}$
\end{proof}

We can now finish the proof: let $\alpha$ be as in \ref{NC:SomeThetaPreservesProjectiveStationarity}. In $W$, let ${\mathord{\mathbb Q}}=\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}}$.
By Lemma \ref{lem:PreservationOfMediatedProjectiveStationarity}, in $W$, $\mathbb{T}_{S_{{\ge}\alpha}}\times{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}$ is stationary set preserving. Now back in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$, let $\dot{\mathbb{T}}$ be a ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$-name for $\mathbb{T}_{S_{{\ge}\alpha}}$, and let $\dot{{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}}$ be a ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$-name for ${\mathord{\mathbb Q}}$. Then ${\mathord{\mathbb P}} * ( \dot{{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}} \times \dot{\mathbb{T}} )$ is stationary set preserving. Also, by \cite[Claim 3.13]{Fuchs-LambieHanson:SeparatingDSR}, ${\mathord{\mathbb P}} * \dot{\mathbb{T}}$ has an $\omega_2$-directed closed dense subset. So by Theorem \ref{thm:AbstractPreservation}, $\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}_{\omega_1}(\{{\mathord{\mathbb Q}}\})$ holds in $W$.
