 We still have to deal with the case that $\mu<\lambda$. This will be a straightforward, but somewhat tedious construction.

 Let $\dot{X}'$ be the canonical $\mathbb{A}$-name for the set of all $x\in\dot{X}\mathop{\uparrow}[H_{\check{\lambda}}]^\omega$ that are closed under the function $z\mapsto z\cap H_{\check{\mu}}$. That is, whenever $I\subseteq\mathbb{A}$ is generic over $W$, then
 \begin{ea*}
(\dot{X}')^I=\{x\subseteq H_\lambda^{W[I]}&\;|\;&\text{$x$ is countable in $W[I]$, $x\cap H^{W[I]}_\mu\in \dot{X}^I$ and}\\ 
&&\text{for all $z\in x$, $z\cap H_\mu^{W[I]}\in x$}\}.
\end{ea*}

 \begin{NumberedClaim}
 \label{NC:A*PS'IsSSP}
 	$\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}'}$ is stationary set preserving.
 \end{NumberedClaim}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \ref{NC:A*PS'IsSSP}.]

Since $\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}}$ is stationary set preserving, we know by Observation \ref{obs:MediatedProjectiveStationarity} that $\mathbb{A}$ forces that $\dot{X}_{\check{T}}$ is stationary, for any stationary $T\subseteq\omega_1$.
So $\mathbb{A}$ forces that $\dot{X}_{\check{T}}\mathop{\uparrow}[H_{\check{\lambda}}]^\omega$ is stationary.

Let $\dot{Z}$ be a name for the set $\{x\in[H_{\check{\lambda}}]^\omega\;|\;\text{$x$ is closed under $z\mapsto z\cap H_{\check{\mu}}$}\}$. Since this $\dot{Z}$ is forced to be club in $[H_{\check{\lambda}}]^\omega$, $\mathbb{A}$ forces that $\dot{X}_{\check{T}}\mathop{\uparrow}[H_{\check{\lambda}}]^\omega\cap\dot{Z}$ is stationary. But $\mathbb{A}$ forces that
$\dot{X}_{\check{T}}\mathop{\uparrow}[H_{\check{\lambda}}]^\omega\cap\dot{Z}  
                                                                  
 = (\dot{X}\mathop{\uparrow}[H_{\check{\lambda}}]^\omega\cap\dot{Z})_{\check{T}}
 =\dot{X}'_{\check{T}}$.
Thus, $\mathbb{A}$ forces that $\dot{X}'_{\check{T}}$ is stationary, for every stationary $T\subseteq\omega_1$. By Observation \ref{obs:MediatedProjectiveStationarity} again, this means that $\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}'}$ is stationary set preserving.
  \hspace*{\fill}$\qedhere_{\ref{NC:A*PS'IsSSP}}$\end{proof}

Hence, by the above argument, we have:

\begin{NumberedClaim}
\label{NC:FAforA*PS'}
$\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}_{\omega_1}(\{\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}'}\})$ holds.
\end{NumberedClaim}

We will argue that this implies $\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}_{\omega_1}(\{\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}}\})$. To this end, we devise a way to lift an arbitrary dense set $D$ of conditions in $\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}}$ to a dense subset $D'$ of  $\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}'}$.

First, if $ {\langle a,\dot{p}' \rangle} \in\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}'}$, then whenever $I\subseteq\mathbb{A}$ is generic over $W$ with $a\in I$, $(\dot{p}')^I$ is an increasing continuous $\in$-sequence ${\vec{x}}'={\langlex'_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi\le\zeta\rangle}$, for some $\zeta<\omega_1$, with $x'_\xi\in X'=(\dot{X}')^I$, and we can consider in $W[I]$ the sequence ${\vec{x}}={\langlex_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi\le\zeta\rangle}$ defined by $x_\xi=x'_\xi\cap H_\mu^{W[I]}$, for $\xi\le\zeta$. Then ${\vec{x}}$ is an increasing continuous $\in$-chain: being increasing continuous is obvious, and for $\xi\leq\zeta$, we have $x'_\xi\in x'_{\xi+1}$, so since $x'_{\xi+1}\in X'$ and hence $x'_{\xi+1}$ is closed under the function $z\mapsto z\cap H_\mu^{W[I]}$, $x_\xi=x'_\xi\cap H_\mu^{W[I]}\in x'_{\xi+1}$. But since $x_\xi$ is a countable subset of $H_\mu^{W[I]}$, and since $\mu$ is a regular uncountable cardinal in $W[I]$, $x_\xi\in H_\mu^{W[I]}$. Thus, $x_\xi\in x'_{\xi+1}\cap H_\mu^{W[I]}=x_{\xi+1}$. Clearly, each $x_\xi$ is in $X=\dot{X}^I$, and so, $\vec{x}\in{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$. This construction can be carried out in $W[I]$ whenever $a\in I$, so there is a canonical name for $\vec{x}$, denote it $ {\langle a,\dot{p}' \rangle} \sqcap H_\mu$.

Returning to our dense set $D\subseteq\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}}$, we can now define
\[D'=\{ {\langle a,\dot{p}' \rangle} \in\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}'}\;|\;\exists\dot{p}\ ( {\langle a,\dot{p} \rangle} \in D\ \land\ a\Vdash_\mathbb{A}\dot{p}= {\langle a,\dot{p}' \rangle} \sqcap H_\mu)\}.\]

\begin{NumberedClaim}
\label{NC:D'isDense}
$D'\subseteq\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}'}$ is dense.
\end{NumberedClaim}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \ref{NC:D'isDense}]
Let $ {\langle a,\dot{p}' \rangle} \in\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}'}$ be given. Let $\dot{p}= {\langle a,\dot{p}' \rangle} \sqcap H_\mu$. So $ {\langle a,\dot{p} \rangle} \in\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}}$. Since $D$ is dense in $\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}}$, we can pick $ {\langle b,\dot{q} \rangle} \le_{\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}}} {\langle a,\dot{p} \rangle} $ in $D$.

Let $I\subseteq\mathbb{A}$ be generic, with $b\in I$. Let ${\vec{x}}'={\langlex'_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi\le\zeta\rangle}=(\dot{p}')^I$, ${\vec{x}}={\langlex_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi\le\zeta\rangle} = \dot{p}^I$ and ${\vec{y}}={\langley_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi\le\zeta^*\rangle} = \dot{q}^I$. Define ${\vec{y}}'={\langley'_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi\le\zeta^*\rangle}$ by letting $y'_\xi=x'_\xi$ for $\xi\le\zeta$.
To define $y'_\xi$ for $\zeta<\xi\le\zeta^*$, suppose $y'_\eta$ has already been defined for $\eta<\xi$. If $\xi$ is a limit ordinal, then let $y'_\xi=\bigcup_{\eta<\xi}y'_\eta$. Now suppose $\xi=\eta+1$. Then let $y'_\xi=y_\xi\cup y'_\eta \cup \{ y'_\eta \}$.

Let $X=\dot{X}^I$, $X'=(\dot{X}')^I$, $H=H_\mu^{W[I]}$. We claim that $\vec{y}'\in{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{X'}$. ${\vec{y}} '$ is increasing continuous by definition. It is an $\in$-chain: let $\xi\leq\zeta^*$. If $\xi<\zeta$, then $y'_\xi=x'_\xi\in x'_{\xi+1}=y'_{\xi+1}$. If $\zeta\leq\xi<\zeta^*$ then $y'_\xi\in y'_{\xi+1}$ by definition.

Further, for $\xi\leq\zeta^*$, $y'_\xi\cap H=y_\xi$: this is clear for $\xi\le\zeta$ since then $y'_\xi\cap H=x'_\xi\cap H=x_\xi=y_\xi$. If $\zeta<\xi\le\zeta^*$, then we proceed by induction. If $\xi$ is a limit ordinal then it is clear by the continuity of ${\vec{y}}$. So suppose $\xi=\eta +1$. Then $y'_\xi\cap H=(y_\xi\cup y'_\eta \cup \{ y'_\eta \})\cap H$. Now there are two possibilities. First, it could be that $y'_\eta\in H$. In this case $y'_\eta=y_\eta$, and it follows that $y'_\xi=y_\xi\cup y'_\eta \cup\{ y'_\eta \}=y_\xi\cup y_\eta \cup \{ y_\eta \} = y_\xi$ as $y_\eta \cup \{ y_\eta \} \subseteq y_\xi$. So $y'_\xi \cap H = y_\xi$. Otherwise, $y'_\eta\notin H$. In this case, we have $y'_\xi\cap H=(y_\xi\cup y'_\eta \cup \{ y'_\eta \})\cap H= y_\xi \cup y_\eta = y_\xi$, as claimed.

So, since $y_\xi\in X$, $y'_\xi\in X\mathop{\uparrow}([H_\lambda]^\omega)^{W[I]}$.
Moreover, $y'_\xi$ is closed under $z\mapsto z\cap H$: we proceed by induction. This is clear for $\xi\le\zeta$, since then, $y'_\xi=x'_\xi$. It is also clear for limit $\xi$. So suppose $\xi=\eta +1$. Let $z\in y'_\xi=y_\xi\cup y'_\eta \cup \{ y'_\eta \}$. If $z\in y_\xi$, then $z\cap H=z\in y_\xi \subseteq y'_\xi$. If $z \in y'_\eta$, then, by the induction hypothesis, $z\cap H \in y'_\eta \subseteq y'_\xi$. If $z = y'_\eta$, then $z \cap H = y'_\eta\cap H=y_\eta\in y_\xi \subseteq y'_\xi$.

Hence, ${\vec{y}}'\in{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{X'}$, and obviously, ${\vec{y}}'\le_{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{X'}}{\vec{x}}'$.

The construction of ${\vec{y}}'$ can be carried out in $W[I]$ whenever $b\in I$, so there is an $\mathbb{A}$-name for ${\vec{y}}'$, call it $\dot{q}'$. Thus, we have that $b\Vdash_\mathbb{A}\dot{q}'\le_{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}'}}\dot{p}'$, so that $ {\langle b,\dot{q}' \rangle} \le_{\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}'}} {\langle a,\dot{p}' \rangle} $. Further, $ {\langle b,\dot{q}' \rangle} \in D'$, as witnessed by $\dot{q}$, since $ {\langle b,\dot{q} \rangle} \in D$, and for $\xi\le\zeta^*$, $y'_\xi\cap H=y_\xi$. \hfill$\qedhere_{\ref{NC:D'isDense}}$
\end{proof}

Now, let $\Delta$ be a collection of $\omega_1$ dense subsets of $\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}}$, and let $\Delta'=\{D'\;|\; D\in\Delta\}$. Then $\Delta'$ is a collection of $\omega_1$ dense subsets of $\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}'}$, so by \ref{NC:FAforA*PS'}, there is a $\Delta'$-generic filter $F'\subseteq\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}'}$. Let
\[
F=\{ {\langle a,\dot{p} \rangle} 
\;|\;\exists\dot{p}'\ ( {\langle a,\dot{p}' \rangle} \in F'
\land a\Vdash_\mathbb{A} \dot{p}= {\langle a,\dot{p}' \rangle} \sqcap H_\mu\}.
\]
It is not hard to see that $F$ is a filter in $\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}}$. Moreover, it is $\Delta$-generic, because given $D\in\Delta$, let $ {\langle a,\dot{p}' \rangle} \in D'\cap F'$. By definition of $D'$, let $\dot{p}$ be such that $ {\langle a,\dot{p} \rangle} \in D$ and $a$ forces that $\dot{p}= {\langle a,\dot{p}' \rangle} \sqcap H_\mu$. Then $ {\langle a,\dot{p} \rangle} \in F$, as witnessed by $\dot{p}'$.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
If not, then let $I\subseteq\mathbb{A}$ be generic such that, letting $H=\dot{H}^I$ and $X=\dot{X}^I$, in $W[I]$, for every $\alpha<\lambda$, there is a club $Z_\alpha\subseteq[H]^\omega$ disjoint from $X\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S_{{\ge}\alpha},[H]^\omega)$. That is, for all $\alpha<\lambda$, $X\cap Z_\alpha\subseteq\mathsf{lifting}(S_{{\ge}\alpha},[H]^\omega)$. By the normality of the generalized club filter (see \cite[Lemma 8.23]{ST3}), let $Z\subseteq[H]^\omega$ be club such that for all $x\in Z$, for all $\alpha\in x\cap\lambda$, $x\in Z_\alpha$. Pick $x\in X\cap Z$, and let $\gamma=\sup(x\cap\lambda)<\lambda$. Then, for all $\alpha\in x\cap\lambda$, $x\in Z_\alpha$, and hence, since $x\in X$, $x\in\mathsf{lifting}(S_{{\ge}\alpha},[H]^\omega)$, which means that $\gamma=\sup(x\cap\lambda)\in S_{{\ge}\alpha}$. Since the $S_\alpha$'s are pairwise disjoint, and since $x\cap\lambda$ is unbounded in $\gamma$, it follows that $\gamma\in S_{{\ge}\gamma}$. But, say $\delta\ge\gamma$ is such that $\gamma\in S_\delta$. Then $\gamma\in S_\delta\cap(\delta +1)=\emptyset$, a contradiction. \hspace*{\fill}$\qedhere_{\ref{NC:ThereAlwaysIsAnAlpha}}$
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
By \ref{NC:ThereAlwaysIsAnAlpha}, let $\dot{\beta}$ be an $\mathbb{A}$-name such that
\[\Vdash_\mathbb{A}\text{``$\dot{\beta}<\check{\lambda}$ is least such that $\dot{X}\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(\check{S}_{{\ge}\dot{\beta}},[\dot{H}]^\omega)$ is stationary.''}\]
Let $B=\{\beta<\lambda\;|\;\exists p\in\mathbb{A}\quad p\Vdash_\mathbb{A}\dot{\beta}=\check{\beta}\}$, and for each $\beta\in B$, let $p_\beta\Vdash_\mathbb{A}\dot{\beta}=\check{\beta}$. Clearly, if $\beta,\beta ' \in B$ are distinct, then $p_\beta$ and $p_{\beta '}$ are incompatible. So $P=\{p_\beta\;|\;\beta\in P\}$ is an antichain of the same cardinality as $B$. Since $\mathbb{A}$ is $\lambda$-c.c., this cardinality is less than $\lambda$, and since $\lambda$ is regular, $\alpha=\sup B<\lambda$. Then $\alpha$ is as desired, because if $I\subseteq\mathbb{A}$ is generic, then by \ref{NC:ThereAlwaysIsAnAlpha}, there is a minimal $\beta<\lambda$ such that in $W[I]$, $\dot{X}^I\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S_{{\ge}\beta},[\dot{H}^I]^\omega)$ is stationary. But then, $\beta\in B$, and hence, $\beta\le\alpha$. So $S_{{\ge}\alpha}\subseteq S_{{\ge}\beta}$, and hence, in $W[I]$, $\dot{X}^I\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S_{{\ge}\alpha},[\dot{H}^I]^\omega)$ is stationary as well. \hspace*{\fill}$\qedhere_{\ref{NC:AFixedThetaAlwaysWorks}}$
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
By assumption, $\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}}$ is stationary set preserving, and hence, so is $\mathbb{A}$. By Observation \ref{obs:MediatedProjectiveStationarity}, this means that for every stationary $T\subseteq\omega_1$, $\Vdash_\mathbb{A}\text{``$\dot{X}_{\check{T}}$ is stationary.''}$ Now, fix such a stationary $T$. By \ref{NC:AFixedThetaAlwaysWorks}, there is a $\alpha_T$ such that
\[\Vdash_\mathbb{A}\text{``$\dot{X}_{\check{T}}\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(\check{A}_{{\ge}\alpha_T},[\dot{H}]^\omega)$ is stationary.''}\]
Since $2^{\omega_1}=\omega_2<\lambda$, $\alpha=\sup\{\alpha_T\;|\; T\subseteq\omega_1\ \text{is stationary}\}<\lambda$. It follows as before that $\alpha$ is as wished. \hspace*{\fill}$\qedhere_{\ref{NC:SomeThetaPreservesProjectiveStationarity}}$
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
Since $\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}}$ is stationary set preserving, we know by Observation \ref{obs:MediatedProjectiveStationarity} that $\mathbb{A}$ forces that $\dot{X}_{\check{T}}$ is stationary, for any stationary $T\subseteq\omega_1$.
So $\mathbb{A}$ forces that $\dot{X}_{\check{T}}\mathop{\uparrow}[H_{\check{\lambda}}]^\omega$ is stationary.

Let $\dot{Z}$ be a name for the set $\{x\in[H_{\check{\lambda}}]^\omega\;|\;\text{$x$ is closed under $z\mapsto z\cap H_{\check{\mu}}$}\}$. Since this $\dot{Z}$ is forced to be club in $[H_{\check{\lambda}}]^\omega$, $\mathbb{A}$ forces that $\dot{X}_{\check{T}}\mathop{\uparrow}[H_{\check{\lambda}}]^\omega\cap\dot{Z}$ is stationary. But $\mathbb{A}$ forces that
$\dot{X}_{\check{T}}\mathop{\uparrow}[H_{\check{\lambda}}]^\omega\cap\dot{Z}  
                                                                  
 = (\dot{X}\mathop{\uparrow}[H_{\check{\lambda}}]^\omega\cap\dot{Z})_{\check{T}}
 =\dot{X}'_{\check{T}}$.
Thus, $\mathbb{A}$ forces that $\dot{X}'_{\check{T}}$ is stationary, for every stationary $T\subseteq\omega_1$. By Observation \ref{obs:MediatedProjectiveStationarity} again, this means that $\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}'}$ is stationary set preserving.
  \hspace*{\fill}$\qedhere_{\ref{NC:A*PS'IsSSP}}$
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
Let $ {\langle a,\dot{p}' \rangle} \in\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}'}$ be given. Let $\dot{p}= {\langle a,\dot{p}' \rangle} \sqcap H_\mu$. So $ {\langle a,\dot{p} \rangle} \in\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}}$. Since $D$ is dense in $\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}}$, we can pick $ {\langle b,\dot{q} \rangle} \le_{\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}}} {\langle a,\dot{p} \rangle} $ in $D$.

Let $I\subseteq\mathbb{A}$ be generic, with $b\in I$. Let ${\vec{x}}'={\langlex'_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi\le\zeta\rangle}=(\dot{p}')^I$, ${\vec{x}}={\langlex_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi\le\zeta\rangle} = \dot{p}^I$ and ${\vec{y}}={\langley_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi\le\zeta^*\rangle} = \dot{q}^I$. Define ${\vec{y}}'={\langley'_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi\le\zeta^*\rangle}$ by letting $y'_\xi=x'_\xi$ for $\xi\le\zeta$.
To define $y'_\xi$ for $\zeta<\xi\le\zeta^*$, suppose $y'_\eta$ has already been defined for $\eta<\xi$. If $\xi$ is a limit ordinal, then let $y'_\xi=\bigcup_{\eta<\xi}y'_\eta$. Now suppose $\xi=\eta+1$. Then let $y'_\xi=y_\xi\cup y'_\eta \cup \{ y'_\eta \}$.

Let $X=\dot{X}^I$, $X'=(\dot{X}')^I$, $H=H_\mu^{W[I]}$. We claim that $\vec{y}'\in{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{X'}$. ${\vec{y}} '$ is increasing continuous by definition. It is an $\in$-chain: let $\xi\leq\zeta^*$. If $\xi<\zeta$, then $y'_\xi=x'_\xi\in x'_{\xi+1}=y'_{\xi+1}$. If $\zeta\leq\xi<\zeta^*$ then $y'_\xi\in y'_{\xi+1}$ by definition.

Further, for $\xi\leq\zeta^*$, $y'_\xi\cap H=y_\xi$: this is clear for $\xi\le\zeta$ since then $y'_\xi\cap H=x'_\xi\cap H=x_\xi=y_\xi$. If $\zeta<\xi\le\zeta^*$, then we proceed by induction. If $\xi$ is a limit ordinal then it is clear by the continuity of ${\vec{y}}$. So suppose $\xi=\eta +1$. Then $y'_\xi\cap H=(y_\xi\cup y'_\eta \cup \{ y'_\eta \})\cap H$. Now there are two possibilities. First, it could be that $y'_\eta\in H$. In this case $y'_\eta=y_\eta$, and it follows that $y'_\xi=y_\xi\cup y'_\eta \cup\{ y'_\eta \}=y_\xi\cup y_\eta \cup \{ y_\eta \} = y_\xi$ as $y_\eta \cup \{ y_\eta \} \subseteq y_\xi$. So $y'_\xi \cap H = y_\xi$. Otherwise, $y'_\eta\notin H$. In this case, we have $y'_\xi\cap H=(y_\xi\cup y'_\eta \cup \{ y'_\eta \})\cap H= y_\xi \cup y_\eta = y_\xi$, as claimed.

So, since $y_\xi\in X$, $y'_\xi\in X\mathop{\uparrow}([H_\lambda]^\omega)^{W[I]}$.
Moreover, $y'_\xi$ is closed under $z\mapsto z\cap H$: we proceed by induction. This is clear for $\xi\le\zeta$, since then, $y'_\xi=x'_\xi$. It is also clear for limit $\xi$. So suppose $\xi=\eta +1$. Let $z\in y'_\xi=y_\xi\cup y'_\eta \cup \{ y'_\eta \}$. If $z\in y_\xi$, then $z\cap H=z\in y_\xi \subseteq y'_\xi$. If $z \in y'_\eta$, then, by the induction hypothesis, $z\cap H \in y'_\eta \subseteq y'_\xi$. If $z = y'_\eta$, then $z \cap H = y'_\eta\cap H=y_\eta\in y_\xi \subseteq y'_\xi$.

Hence, ${\vec{y}}'\in{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{X'}$, and obviously, ${\vec{y}}'\le_{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{X'}}{\vec{x}}'$.

The construction of ${\vec{y}}'$ can be carried out in $W[I]$ whenever $b\in I$, so there is an $\mathbb{A}$-name for ${\vec{y}}'$, call it $\dot{q}'$. Thus, we have that $b\Vdash_\mathbb{A}\dot{q}'\le_{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}'}}\dot{p}'$, so that $ {\langle b,\dot{q}' \rangle} \le_{\mathbb{A}*{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_{\dot{X}'}} {\langle a,\dot{p}' \rangle} $. Further, $ {\langle b,\dot{q}' \rangle} \in D'$, as witnessed by $\dot{q}$, since $ {\langle b,\dot{q} \rangle} \in D$, and for $\xi\le\zeta^*$, $y'_\xi\cap H=y_\xi$. \hfill$\qedhere_{\ref{NC:D'isDense}}$
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- defn-18: body_md -->
The principle $\diamondsuit(\omega_1^{{<}\omega})$ states 
\footnote{This is a strengthening due to Lietz of a principle of the same name introduced earlier by Woodin \cite[Def.~6.37]{Woodin:ADforcingAxiomsNonstationaryIdeal}.}
that there exists a function $f:\omega_1\longrightarrow H_{\omega_1}$ such that for every $\zeta<\omega_1$, $f(\zeta)$ is a filter in $\mathrm{Col}(\omega,\zeta)$ and such that for every $b\in\mathrm{Col}(\omega,\omega_1)$ and every regular $\theta>\omega_1$, letting $\mathcal{N} =  {\langle H_\theta , {\in},f \rangle} $, the set
\begin{ea*}
X_{\theta,b,f}=\{M\in[H_\theta]^\omega&\!\;|\;\!& \mathcal{N} | M \prec \mathcal{N} ,\ b\in f(M\cap\omega_1)\\ &&\text{and}\ f(M\cap\omega_1)\ \text{is}\ (M,\mathrm{Col}(\omega,M\cap\omega_1))\text{-generic}\}
\end{ea*} 
is stationary in $[H_\theta]^\omega$. In this situation, we say that $f$ witnesses $\diamondsuit(\omega_1^{{<}\omega})$, and we say that a forcing ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ is $f$-preserving if after forcing with ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$, $f$ still witnesses $\diamondsuit(\omega_1^{{<}\omega})$.
<!-- end defn-18 -->
<!-- defn-19: body_md -->
\label{def:QM}
$\mathsf{QM}$ is the statement saying that there is a function $f$ which witnesses $\diamondsuit(\omega_1^{{<}\omega})$ such that the forcing axiom $\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}_{\omega_1}(\Gamma)$ for the class $\Gamma$ consisting of all $f$-preserving forcing notions holds.
<!-- end defn-19 -->
<!-- thm-7: body_md -->
\label{thm:Lietz}
Assuming the consistency of an inaccessible limit of supercompact cardinals, $\mathsf{QM}$ is consistent. $\mathsf{QM}$ implies $\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace$ and that $\mathsf{NS}_{\omega_1}$ has density $\omega_1$.
<!-- end thm-7 -->
<!-- obs-4: body_md -->
\label{obs:QMpreservation}
$\mathsf{QM}$, if true, is preserved by $\omega_2$-directed closed forcing.
<!-- end obs-4 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
Cox \cite[Theorem 44]{Cox:FAapproachabilitySSR} states that for any forcing class $\Gamma$ which is closed under restrictions and under 2-step iterations, $\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}_{\omega_1}(\Gamma)$ is preserved by $\omega_2$-directed closed forcing. The class of $f$-preserving forcing notions obviously has these two properties, but actually, the argument of \cite[Theorem 44]{Cox:FAapproachabilitySSR} also needs that the class in question contains every $\omega_2$-directed closed forcing notion, and that it is closed under forcing equivalence. Since it is obvious that the class of $f$-preserving forcing is closed under forcing equivalence, let us quickly check that it contains the $\omega_2$-directed closed forcings. In fact, it contains all $\omega_2$-closed forcings.

To see this, let $f$ witness that $\diamondsuit(\omega_1^{{<}\omega})$ holds, let ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ be $\omega_2$-closed, and let $G$ be ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$-generic over $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$. Fix a condition $b\in\mathrm{Col}(\omega,\omega_1)$ and a regular cardinal $\theta>\omega_1$ in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]$. We have to show that $(X_{\theta,b,f})^{\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]}$ is stationary in $[H_\theta^{\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]}]^\omega$ (in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]$). But since $f$ is a $\diamondsuit(\omega_1^{{<}\omega})$-sequence, we know that ${\bar{X}}=(X_{\theta,b,f})^V$ is stationary in $[H_\theta^\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace]^\omega$ (in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$). Since ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ is proper, it preserves this property, so ${\bar{X}}$ is a stationary subset of $[H_\theta^\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace]^\omega$ also in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]$. So, working in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]$,
\[X={\bar{X}}\mathop{\uparrow}[H_\theta^{\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]}]^\omega=\{N\in[H_\theta^{\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]}]^\omega\;|\; N\cap H_\theta^\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace\in{\bar{X}}\}\]
is stationary in $[H_\theta^{\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]}]^\omega$. So the set $X'=\{N\in X \;|\;  {\langle N,{\in} \rangle} \prec  {\langle H_\theta^{\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]},{\in} \rangle} \}$ is stationary, too.

But $X'\subseteq(X_{\theta,b,f})^{\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]}$: let $N\in X'$ and $M=N\cap H_\theta^\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$. Since $M\in{\bar{X}}$, $f\in M\subseteq N$. Further, let $\zeta =M\cap\omega_1=N\cap\omega_1$. Since $M\in{\bar{X}}$, $b\in f(\zeta)=f(N\cap\omega_1)$. Finally, let's check that $f(\zeta)$ is $(N,\mathrm{Col}(\omega,\omega_1))$-generic. Working in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]$, let $D\subseteq\mathrm{Col}(\omega,\omega_1)$ be a dense subset, $D\in N$. Since ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ is $\omega_2$-closed, $D\in\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$. So actually, $D\in N\cap H_\theta^\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace =M$. So, since $f(\zeta)$ is $(M,\mathrm{Col}(\omega,\delta))$-generic, there is a $c\in f(\zeta)\cap D\cap M$. So $c\in f(\zeta)\cap D \cap N$.

Thus, $X'\subseteq(X_{\theta,b,f})^{\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]}$, and hence, the latter is stationary in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[G]$.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- obs-5: body_md -->
\label{obs:QMimpliesSRPFA}
$\mathsf{QM}$ implies $\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}$.
<!-- end obs-5 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
By $\mathsf{QM}$, let $f$ be a $\diamondsuit(\omega_1^{{<}\omega})$ witness such that the forcing axiom for all $f$-preserving forcing notions holds. We will have to use some concepts introduced in \cite{Lietz:NSdense} without explaining their meaning. Thus, by \cite[Lemma 7.5]{Lietz:NSdense}), $\ensuremath{\mathsf{MM}}\xspace(f)$ holds, and this  implies $\mathsf{SPFA}(f)$ (they are actually equivalent, by \cite[Thm.~3.67(a)]{Lietz:NSdense}). Now, Lietz \cite[Lemma 3.69]{Lietz:NSdense} shows that $\mathsf{SPFA}(f)$ implies \ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace. We will argue that actually, $\mathsf{SPFA}(f)$ implies $\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}$ (so our observation is actually slightly stronger than stated).

To this end, let $X\subseteq[H_\theta]^\omega$ be projective stationary. We claim that ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ is $f$-semiproper (see \cite[Def.~3.31]{Lietz:NSdense}). We already know by \cite[Lemma 3.69]{Lietz:NSdense} that \ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace holds. This implies the reflection principle $\mathsf{RP}$ of \cite[Thm 37.23]{ST3}. This principle $\mathsf{RP}$, in turn, implies the statement $(\dagger)$ that every stationary set preserving forcing is semiproper; see \cite[Ex.~37.13]{ST3}. But $(\dagger)$ implies that being projective stationary is equivalent to being  spanning (\cite[Thm. 4.4]{FengJechZapletal:StructureOfStationarySets}). Thus, our set $X$ is spanning, meaning that for every $\lambda>2^\theta$ and every club $Z\subseteq[H_\lambda]^\omega$ there is a club $Y\subseteq[H_\lambda]^\omega$ such that for every $M\in Y$ there is a $N\in Z$ such that $N\cap H_\theta\in X$, $M\subseteq N$ and $M\cap\omega_1=N\cap\omega_1$. 

To show that ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ is $f$-semiproper, closely following the proof of \cite[Lemma 3.69]{Lietz:NSdense}, we fix a condition $p\in{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ and some regular $\lambda>2^\theta$, and we let $Z$ be the club of all $M \in [H_\lambda]^\omega$ with $ {\langle M,{\in},\Delta {\restriction} M \rangle}  \prec  {\langle H_\lambda,{\in},\Delta \rangle} $, where $\Delta$ is a well-ordering of $H_\lambda$. Let $Y$ be a club subset of $[H_\lambda]^\omega$ as in the definition of being spanning. Let $M\in Y$ be $f$-slim, with ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X,f,p\in M$. We have to find a $q\le p$ which is $(M,{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X,f)$-semigeneric.

This can be done as in the proof of \cite[Lemma 3.69]{Lietz:NSdense}: let $N\in Z$ be such that $M\cap\omega_1=N\cap\omega_1$, $M\subseteq N$ and $N\cap H_\theta\in X$. Letting $\zeta=M\cap\omega_1=N\cap\omega_1$, it follows by \cite[Thm.~3.30]{Lietz:NSdense} that $f(\zeta)$ is $\mathrm{Col}(\omega,\zeta)$-generic over ${\bar{N}}$, the Mostowski collapse of $N$. Let $\sigma : {\bar{N}} \longrightarrow N$ be the inverse of the Mostowski collapsing map. Let ${\langle{\bar{D}}_n\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{n<\omega\rangle}$ be an enumeration of all dense subsets of $\sigma^{-1} ( {\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X )$ in ${\bar{N}}[ f( \zeta ) ]$, and take a decreasing sequence ${\langle{\bar{p}}_n\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{n<\omega\rangle}$ in $\sigma^{-1} ( {\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X )$ with ${\bar{p}}_n \in {\bar{D}}_n$ for all $n < \omega$. Let $p_n = \sigma ( {\bar{p}}_n )$ for $n<\omega$. Then $\bigcup_{n<\omega}p_n$ is of the form ${\langleN_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi<\zeta\rangle}$, and $N_\zeta:=\bigcup_{\xi<\zeta}N_\xi=N\cap H_\theta\in X$. So $q={\langleN_\xi\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\xi\le\zeta\rangle}$ is a condition in ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$, and it follows as outlined at the end of the proof of  \cite[Lemma 3.69]{Lietz:NSdense} that $q\le p$ is $(M,{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X,f)$-semigeneric. 

Thus, by $\ensuremath{\mathsf{SPFA}}\xspace(f)$, $\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}_{\omega_1}(\{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X\})$ holds.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- cor-2: body_md -->
\label{cor:AssumptionConsistent}
If it is consistent that there is an inaccessible limit of supercompact cardinals, then it is consistent that the density of $\mathsf{NS}_{\omega_1}$ is $\omega_1$, and that $\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}$ holds in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$ as well as in every forcing extension of $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$ by an $\omega_2$-directed closed forcing notion.
<!-- end cor-2 -->
<!-- thm-8: body_md -->
\label{thm:INSdenseuDSRfailsAtOmega2}
If the density of $\mathsf{NS}_{\omega_1}$ is $\omega_1$ and $\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}$ holds and continues to hold in any forcing extension by an $\omega_2$-directed closed forcing notion, then there is an $\omega_2$-strategically closed forcing notion which produces forcing extensions where $\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}$ holds yet $\mathsf{uDSR}(1,S^{\omega_2}_\omega)$ fails.
<!-- end thm-8 -->
<!-- head-4: body_md -->
As mentioned before, the principles $\ensuremath{\mathsf{OSR}}\xspace_{\omega_2}$, $\mathsf{uDSR}(\omega_1,S^{\omega_2}_\omega)$ and $\mathsf{DSR}(\omega_1,S^{\omega_2}_\omega)$ are equivalent; see Fuchs \& Lambie-Hanson \cite[Lemma 2.4]{Fuchs-LambieHanson:SeparatingDSR}. Furthermore, \cite[Thm.~3.15]{Fuchs-LambieHanson:SeparatingDSR} shows that $\mathsf{uDSR}({<}\omega_1,S^{\omega_2}_\omega)$ does not imply $\ensuremath{\mathsf{sDSR}}(1,S^{\omega_2}_\omega)$, and \cite[Cor. 3.20]{Fuchs-LambieHanson:SeparatingDSR} shows that $\ensuremath{\mathsf{sDSR}}({<}\omega,S^{\omega_2}_\omega)$ does not imply $\mathsf{DSR}(1,S^{\omega_2}_\omega)$ (and the latter is a consequence of $\ensuremath{\mathsf{OSR}}\xspace_{\omega_2})$. Hence, this theorem provides a stronger separation result than Theorem \ref{thm:ALaLarson}.
<!-- end head-4 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
By assumption, we may fix an $\omega_1$-sized collection $\mathcal{T}$ of stationary subsets of $\omega_1$ which is dense in the stationary subsets of $\omega_1$, that is, for every stationary $U\subseteq\omega_1$, there is a $T\in\mathcal{T}$ such that $T\setminus U$ is nonstationary. In the original proof, it was carelessly stated that an $\omega_1$-sized collection $\mathcal{T}$ of stationary subsets of $\omega_1$ can be found such that for every stationary $U\subseteq\omega_1$ there is an $T\in\mathcal{T}$ such that $T\subseteq U$, but this is easily seen to be impossible - in fact, it is easy to see that for any collection of $\omega_1$ many unbounded subsets of $\omega_1$, there is a club subset of $\omega_1$ that is not a superset of any set from the given collection. But the proof goes through without difficulty, as we shall see.

Let ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ be the $\omega_2$-strategically closed forcing used in the proof of \cite[Theorem 3.10]{Fuchs-LambieHanson:SeparatingDSR} to add a counterexample to $\mathsf{uDSR}(1,S^{\omega_2}_\omega)$, by initial segments. It is the same forcing we already used in the proof of Theorem \ref{thm:EnhancedSRPDoesntImplyuDSR}, but this time at $\omega_2$. It adds a sequence ${\langleS_\alpha\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha<\omega_2\rangle}$ of pairwise disjoint stationary subsets of $S^{\omega_2}_\omega$ such that $S_\alpha\cap(\alpha+1)=\emptyset$ and such that for every $\rho<\omega_2$ of uncountable cofinality, the set $\{\alpha<\rho\;|\; S_\alpha\cap\rho\ \text{is stationary in}\ \rho\}$ is bounded in $\rho$. It remains to show that $\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}$ holds in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[{\vec{S}}]$.

Working in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[\vec{S}]$, define $S_{{\ge}\alpha}=\bigcup_{\alpha\le\beta<\omega_2}S_\beta$, and let $\mathbb{T}_\alpha$ be the forcing $\mathbb{T}_{S_{{\ge}\alpha}}$ of Definition \ref{def:KillingStationarySets}, shooting a club through the complement of $S_{{\ge}\alpha}$, thus killing the stationarity of $S_{{\ge}\alpha}$. Letting $\dot{\mathbb{T}}_\alpha$ be a canonical ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$-name for $\mathbb{T}_\alpha$,  \cite[Theorem 3.10, Claim 3.13]{Fuchs-LambieHanson:SeparatingDSR} shows that ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}*\dot{\mathbb{T}}_\alpha$ contains an $\omega_2$-directed closed subset of size $\omega_2$ (using that under our assumptions, $\omega_2^{\omega_1}=\omega_2$.) Moreover, by \cite[Theorem 4.4, Claim 4.5]{Fuchs-LambieHanson:SeparatingDSR}, we have that in $V[{\vec{S}}]$, for every stationary $X\subseteq [H_\theta]^\omega$, where $\theta\ge\omega_2$, there is an $\alpha<\omega_2$ such that $\mathbb{T}_\alpha$ preserves the stationarity of $X$. Note also that since ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ does not add new subsets of $\omega_1$, $\mathcal{T}$ is still dense in the stationary subsets of $\omega_1$ from the point of view of $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[{\vec{S}}]$. 

We use these facts in order to show that $\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}$ holds in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[{\vec{S}}]$. To this end, working in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[{\vec{S}}]$, let $\theta\ge\omega_2$ be regular, and let $X\subseteq[H_\theta]^\omega$ be projective stationary. We will find an $\alpha^*<\omega_2$ such that in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[\vec{S}]$, $\mathbb{T}_{\alpha^*}\times{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ is stationary set preserving. By Lemma \ref{lem:WhenT_APreservesGenStationarity} and \ref{lem:PreservationOfP_SbeingSSP}, for this, it suffices to find an $\alpha^*$ such that for every stationary $U\subseteq\omega_1$, $\mathbb{T}_{\alpha^*}$ preserves the stationarity of $X_U=\{x\in X\;|\; x\cap\omega_1\in U\}$.                                                                                                                                                 
To find such an $\alpha^*$, first, for every stationary $U\subseteq\omega_1$, $X_U$ is stationary by the projective stationarity of $X$, so by the above property, we can let $\alpha_U<\omega_2$ be such that $\mathbb{T}_{\alpha_U}$ preserves the stationarity of $X_U$, and then set $\alpha^*=\sup_{T\in\mathcal{T}}\alpha_T<\omega_2$ (since $\mathsf{card}(\mathcal{T})=\omega_1$.) To see that $\alpha^*$ is as wished, let $U\subseteq\omega_1$ be an arbitrary stationary set. Let $T\in\mathcal{T}$ be such that $T\setminus U$ is not stationary. Since $\alpha_T\le\alpha^*$, $S_{{\ge}\alpha^*}\subseteq S_{{\ge}\alpha_T}$, and hence, \[X_T\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S_{{\ge}\alpha_T},[H_\theta]^\omega)\subseteq X_T\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S_{{\ge}\alpha^*},[H_\theta]^\omega).\]
Since $\mathbb{T}_{\alpha_T}$ preserves the stationarity of $X_T$, the former set is stationary by Lemma \ref{lem:WhenT_APreservesGenStationarity}, hence so is the latter. Now, since $T\setminus U$ is not stationary in $\omega_1$, pick a club $C\subseteq\omega_1$ disjoint from $T\setminus U$, so that $T\cap C\subseteq U$. Let $Z^*$ be the set of all $M\in[H_\theta]^\omega$ with $C\in M$ and $ {\langle M,{\in} \rangle} \prec {\langle H_\theta,{\in} \rangle} $. Then $Z^*$ is a club subset of $[H_\theta]^\omega$, and for all $M\in Z^*$, $M\cap\omega_1\in C$, because $M\cap\omega_1$ is a limit point of $C$. Thus,
\[(X_T\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S_{{\ge}\alpha^*},[H_\theta]^\omega))\cap Z^*\subseteq X_U\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S_{{\ge}\alpha^*},[H_\theta]^\omega),\]
so again, since the former set is stationary, so is the latter. By Lemma \ref{lem:WhenT_APreservesGenStationarity} again, this means that $\mathbb{T}_{\alpha^*}$ preserves the stationarity of $X_U$, and so, $\alpha^*$ is as wished. It follows now from Theorem \ref{thm:AbstractPreservation} that $\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}_{\omega_1}(\{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X\})$ holds in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[\vec{S}]$. Since $X$ was an arbitrary projective stationary set, this shows that $\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}$ holds in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[\vec{S}]$, as claimed.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- question-2: body_md -->
Does $\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace + $``$\mathsf{NS}_{\omega_1}$ is not $\omega_1$-dense'' imply (exact) $\mathsf{uDSR}(1,S^{\omega_2}_\omega)$?
<!-- end question-2 -->
<!-- lem-10: body_md -->
\label{lem:whent_apreversesprojstationarity}
Let $\theta\ge\lambda>\omega_1$ be regular cardinals, $S\subseteq \lambda$ a stationary set of ordinals and $X\subseteq[H_\theta]^\omega$ projective stationary, such that $\lambda\setminus S$ is unbounded in $\lambda$ and $\mathbb{T}_S$ is countably distributive.                                              
Then the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\arabic*)}]
\item
\label{item:SminusAisProjStationary}
$X\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S,[H_\theta]^\omega)$ is projective stationary.
      
                                            
                                                         
\item
\label{item:ProductIsSSP}
                                                                                           
$\mathbb{T}_S\times{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ is stationary set preserving.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lem-10 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
\ref{item:SminusAisProjStationary}$\implies$\ref{item:ProductIsSSP}: Assumption  \ref{item:SminusAisProjStationary} implies that for every stationary $T\subseteq\omega_1$, $X_T\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S,[H_\theta]^\omega)$ is stationary. Thus, by Lemma \ref{lem:PreservationOfP_SbeingSSP}, $\mathbb{T}_S\times{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ is stationary set preserving.

\ref{item:ProductIsSSP}$\implies$\ref{item:SminusAisProjStationary}: Fixing a stationary $T\subseteq\omega_1$, we must show that $X_T\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S,[H_\theta]^\omega)$ is stationary. Suppose this is not the case. Then let $Z\subseteq[H_\theta]^\omega$ be club and disjoint from this set, that is, $X_T\cap Z\subseteq\mathsf{lifting}(S,[H_\theta]^\omega)$. 

Let ${\vec{M}}$ be a ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$-generic sequence over $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$. By Fact \ref{fact:P_SisCountablyDistributiveAndExhaustive}, it is an increasing continuous $\in$-chain of elements of $X$ whose union is $H_\theta^\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$, and in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[{\vec{M}}]$, the cofinality of $\lambda$ is $\omega_1$.
Now let $I$ be $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[{\vec{M}}]$-generic for $\mathbb{T}_S$. Let $B=\bigcup I$, so $B$ is the club subset of $\lambda\setminus S$ added by $I$.
Note that since ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X\times\mathbb{T}_S$ is stationary set preserving, $\omega_1^\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$ is still a cardinal in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[{\vec{M}}][I]$, and hence, $\lambda$ still has uncountable cofinality in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[{\vec{M}}][I]$. 

Thus, letting
\[C_0=\{\xi<\omega_1\;|\;\sup(M_\xi\cap\lambda)\in B\},\]
$C_0$ is club in $\omega_1$. Further, define
\[C_1=\{\xi<\omega_1\;|\; M_\xi\in Z\}.\]
Note that $Z$ is club in $[H_\theta^\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace]^\omega$, since $\mathbb{T}_S \times {\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ is countably distributive. So $C_1$ is club in $\omega_1$ as well. 

By assumption, $\mathbb{T}_S\times{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ is stationary set preserving, so $T$ is stationary in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[I][{\vec{M}}]$. Hence, the set
\[T'=\{\xi<\omega_1\;|\; M_\xi\cap\omega_1\in T\}\]
is also stationary. 

So let $\xi\in C_0 \cap C_1\cap T'$. Then $M_\xi\in X$, as ${\vec{M}}$ is ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$-generic, and $M_\xi\cap\omega_1\in T$, as $\xi\in T'$. Since $\xi\in C_1$, $M_\xi\in Z$. Thus, $M_\xi\in X_T\cap Z$. By choice of $Z$, this means that $M_\xi\in\mathsf{lifting}(S,[H_\theta]^\omega)$, that is, $\sup(M_\xi\cap\lambda)\in S$. But $\xi\in C_0$, so $\sup(M_\xi\cap\lambda)\in B$, and $B\cap S=\emptyset$. This is a contradiction.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- thm-9: body_md -->
\label{thm:WhatWeGet}
Let $\lambda$ be a regular cardinal greater than $\omega_1$, and let $S\subseteq\lambda$ be a stationary set such that $\lambda \setminus S$ is unbounded in $\lambda$ and $\mathbb{T}_S$ is countably distributive. Let $\theta\ge\lambda$ be a cardinal, and let $X\subseteq[H_\theta]^\omega$ be spread out.                                   
Then the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\arabic*)}]
\item
\label{item:SpreadOutOffTheLiftingOfA}
$X\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S,[H_\theta]^\omega)$ is spread out.	
\item
\label{item:CompositionSC}
$\mathbb{T}_S\times{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ is $\infty$-subcomplete.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end thm-9 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
\noindent\ref{item:SpreadOutOffTheLiftingOfA}$\implies$\ref{item:CompositionSC}: Suppose not. Let $\sigma:\bar{\mathcal{N}}\prec \mathcal{N}=\mathcal{L}_\tau^A$ be as in Definition \ref{def:(infinity-)subcompleteness}, with ${\bar{G}}\times{\bar{H}}$ generic for $\bar{\mathbb{T}}_{{\bar{S}}}\times\bar{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}}_{\bar{X}}$, the preimage of $\mathbb{T}_S\times{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ under $\sigma$, over $\bar{\mathcal{N}}$.

Note that since $\mathbb{T}_S$ is countably distributive, it does not add any conditions to ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ (which consists of countable sequences from $S$), and $\mathbb{T}_S\times{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ is forcing equivalent to $\mathbb{T}_S *\check{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}}_X$. Note also that by Lemma \ref{lem:WhenT_APreservesGenStationarity}, $X$ is stationary in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace^{\mathbb{T}_S}$. So $\mathbb{T}_S * \check{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}}_X$ is countably distributive.

Fix some $\bar{a}\in\bar{\mathcal{N}}$. We are tasked with finding a condition $ {\langle c,p \rangle} \in\mathbb{T}_S\times{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ that forces the existence of a $\sigma':\bar{\mathcal{N}}\prec \mathcal{N}$ with $\sigma'(\bar{a})=\sigma(a)$ such that $\sigma'``{\bar{G}}\times\bar{H}\subseteq\dot{G}\times\dot{H}$, where $\dot{G}$, $\dot{H}$ are the canonical names for the $\mathbb{T}_S$, ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$-generic filters. Since $\mathbb{T}_S \times {\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ is countably distributive, this is equivalent to showing that there is such a $\sigma'$ in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$.

Let $K=\bigcup X=H_\theta$.
By \ref{item:SpreadOutOffTheLiftingOfA}, there is an embedding $\sigma':\bar{\mathcal{N}}\prec \mathcal{N}$ with $\sigma'({\bar{a}})=\sigma({\bar{a}})$ such that $ {\rm ran} (\sigma')\cap K\in X$ and $\alpha=\sup( {\rm ran} (\sigma')\cap\lambda)\in\lambda\setminus S$. Let $c=(\bigcup\sigma'``{\bar{G}})\cup\{\alpha\}$. Then $c\in\mathbb{T}_S$, since $\alpha\notin S$. Let $p=(\bigcup\sigma'``\bar{H}){{}^\frown} {\langle \sigma'``\bar{K} \rangle} $, where $\bar{K}=\sigma^{-1}(K)$ (note that by genericity, $\bar{K}=\bigcup\bigcup\{ {\rm ran} (s)\;|\; s\in\bar{H}\}$). But $\sigma'``\bar{K}= {\rm ran} (\sigma')\cap K \in X$, which means, again, that $p\in{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$. But the condition $ {\langle c,p \rangle} \in\mathbb{T}_S\times{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$ is then as desired: if $G\times H$ is a $\mathbb{T}_S\times{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$-generic filter containing it, then $\sigma'``\bar{G}\times\bar{H}\subseteq G\times H$.

\ref{item:CompositionSC}$\implies$\ref{item:SpreadOutOffTheLiftingOfA}: Let $\mathcal{N}$, $M$, $a$ be as in the definition of being spread out, $\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{L}_\tau^A$. Let $\sigma:\bar{\mathcal{N}}\prec \mathcal{N}$, $M= {\rm ran} (\sigma)$. Let ${\bar{a}}=\sigma^{-1}(a)$. Also, let $K=H_\theta$. We have to find a $\sigma':\bar{\mathcal{N}}\prec \mathcal{N}$ with $\sigma'({\bar{a}})=a$ and $ {\rm ran} (\sigma')\cap K \in X \setminus \mathsf{lifting}(S,[K]^\omega)$.

Denoting preimages under $\sigma$ by adding bars, let ${\bar{G}}\times{\bar{H}}$ be $\bar{\mathbb{T}}_{\bar{S}}\times\bar{{\mathord{\mathbb P}}}_{\bar{X}}$-generic over $\bar{\mathcal{N}}$. By $\infty$-subcompleteness and countable distributivity of $\mathbb{T}_S \times {\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$, let $ {\langle c,p \rangle} \in\mathbb{T}_S\times{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$, $\sigma'$ be such that whenever $G\times H$ is $\mathbb{T}_S\times{\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$-generic over $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace$ with $ {\langle c,p \rangle} \in G\times H$, it follows that $\sigma'``\bar{G}\times\bar{H}\subseteq G\times H$. Since $\sigma'``{\bar{G}}\subseteq G$ in this situation, it must be that $\tilde{c}=\bigcup\sigma'``{\bar{G}}$, a set closed below its supremum, but having no maximum, can be extended by a condition in $\mathbb{T}_S$ (even in $G$). The weakest possible condition to do this is $\tilde{c}\cup\{\sup\tilde{c}\}$. But by genericity of $\bar{G}$, it follows that $\alpha:=\sup\tilde{c}=\sup ( \sigma'``{\bar{\lambda}} )=\sup ( \lambda\cap {\rm ran} (\sigma') )$. So since $\tilde{c}\cup\{\alpha\}$ must be in $\mathbb{T}_S$, it follows that $\alpha\notin S$. Similarly, the sequence $\tilde{s}=\bigcup\sigma'``\bar{H}$ has no last element, but since it is contained in $H$ and has countable length, its extension by one element must be in $H$, and hence in ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$. There is only one possible extension of it, namely it has to be extended by the union of the elements in the sequence. But by genericity of ${\bar{H}}$, that union must be $\sigma'``\bar{K}$, which is the same as $ {\rm ran} (\sigma')\cap K$. So since $\tilde{s}{{}^\frown}( {\rm ran} (\sigma')\cap K)$ is a condition in ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}_X$, it follows that $ {\rm ran} (\sigma')\cap K\in X$. Thus, $ {\rm ran} (\sigma')\cap K \in X\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S,[K]^\omega)$. This shows that the latter set is spread out.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- obs-6: body_md -->
Assume \ensuremath{\mathsf{MM}}\xspace, and let ${\mathord{\mathbb P}}$ be the forcing to add a sequence ${\langleS_\alpha\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha<\omega_3\rangle}$ such that ${\vec{S}}$ witnesses the failure of $\mathsf{uDSR}(1,S^{\omega_3}_\omega)$ (see Theorem \ref{thm:EnhancedSRPDoesntImplyuDSR}). Then, in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[{\vec{S}}]$, the following statements are true:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\arabic*)}]
\item 
\label{item:SRP}
$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}$ holds, hence so does $\infty$-\ensuremath{\mathsf{SC}}\xspace-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace^\ensuremath{\mathsf{FA}}$. 
\item 
\label{item:SRP-Strategy}
for every set $X$ spread out in $[H_\theta]^\omega$ for some regular $\theta\ge\omega_3$, there is an $\alpha<\omega_3$ such that $X\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S_{\ge\alpha},[H_\theta]^\omega)$ is projective stationary.
\item
\label{item:infSCSRP-StrategyFails} 
there is a set $X$ spread out in $[H_{\omega_3}]^\omega$ for which there is no $\alpha<\omega_3$ such that $X\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S_\alpha,[H_{\omega_3}]^\omega)$ is spread out.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end obs-6 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
\ref{item:SRP} follows from the statement of Theorem \ref{thm:EnhancedSRPDoesntImplyuDSR}, and \ref{item:SRP-Strategy} is what the proof of that Theorem shows.

To see \ref{item:infSCSRP-StrategyFails}, working in $\ensuremath{\mathrm{V}}\xspace[{\vec{S}}]$, fix a sequence ${\vec{T}} = {\langleT_\alpha\;|\;}\linebreak[0]{\alpha<\omega_3\rangle}$ of stationary subsets of $S^{\omega_2}_\omega$ such that if $\alpha<\beta<\omega_3$, then $T_\alpha\cap T_\beta$ is not stationary in $\omega_2$. This is possible, because the nonstationary ideal on $\omega_2$, restricted to $S^{\omega_2}_\omega$, is not $\omega_3$-saturated. As in Definition \ref{def:MagicalSets}, let $X\subseteq[H_{\omega_3}]^\omega$  be defined to consist of those $M$ such that $ {\langle M,{\in} \rangle} \prec {\langle H_{\omega_3},{\in} \rangle} $ and such that either there is no $\alpha\in M\cap\omega_3$ with $\sup(M\cap\omega_2)\in T_\alpha$, or there is a (unique) such $\alpha$, and in this case, $\sup(M\cap\omega_3)\in S_\alpha$. It was shown in Lemma \ref{lem:MagicalSetsSpreadOutAboveKappa} that $X$ is spread out (above $\omega_2$); note here that $\omega_2=2^\omega$, so $(\omega_2)^\omega=\omega_2<\omega_3$.

To prove the crucial property of $X$, fix $\alpha<\omega_3$. We show that $X^-=X\setminus\mathsf{lifting}(S_{\alpha},[H_{\omega_3}]^\omega)$ is not spread out. Namely, let $\theta$ be some sufficiently large cardinal, and let $H_\theta\subseteq \mathcal{N}=\mathcal{L}_\tau^A\models\ensuremath{{\ensuremath{\mathsf{ZFC}}\xspace}^-}\xspace$. We can always find a countable $M$ such that $\mathcal{N} | M \prec \mathcal{N}$ is full, $\alpha\in M$ and $\sup(M\cap\omega_2)\in T_\alpha$. Assume $X^-$ were spread out. Then there would be an $M'$ such that $\mathcal{N} | M' \prec \mathcal{N}$, and there is an isomorphism $\pi:\mathcal{N}|M \longrightarrow \mathcal{N}|M'$ that fixes ${\vec{S}} , {\vec{T}}$ and $\alpha$, and $M'\cap H_{\omega_3}\in X^-$. But since $2^\omega=\omega_2$, $M\cap\omega_2=M'\cap\omega_2$, as ${\mathcal{P}}(\omega)\cap M={\mathcal{P}}(\omega)\cap M'$ and every ordinal less than $\omega_2$ can be coded by a real; see \cite[Obs.~2.32]{Fuchs:CanonicalFragmentsOfSRP}. Since $\alpha$ is fixed by $\pi$, so $\alpha\in M'$, and we have $\sup(M'\cap\omega_2)=\sup(M\cap\omega_2)\in T_\alpha$. Then, since $M'\cap H_{\omega_3}\in X$, we have that $\sup(M'\cap\omega_3)\in S_\alpha$, so $M'\notin X^-$, a contradiction.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- question-3: body_md -->
Does $\ensuremath{\infty\text{-}\mathsf{SC}}\xspace$-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace+\ensuremath{\mathsf{CH}}\xspace$ imply (strongly exact) $\mathsf{DSR}(\lambda,S^\lambda_\omega)$ for any/all regular $\lambda>\omega_2$? Does it help to assume $\diamondsuit$? Or $\diamondsuit^+$?
<!-- end question-3 -->
<!-- question-4: body_md -->
Does $\ensuremath{\infty\text{-}\mathsf{SC}}\xspace$-$\ensuremath{\mathsf{SRP}}\xspace + \ensuremath{\mathsf{CH}}\xspace$ imply \ensuremath{\infty\text{-}\mathsf{SCFA}}\xspace?
<!-- end question-4 -->
